A session idle in a `REPEATABLE READ` transaction that had read the table was ended after 5 s with `40001` `terminating connection due to conflict with recovery`, and its next statement found the connection closed. A session holding only a prepared statement on the table, outside a transaction, didn't delay replay (0.0 to 0.3 s) and its next run worked. With the default delay of 30 s, the swap's replay would wait up to 30 s behind any such query.

The design session repeated it with SA's swap and the reset on the primary and `max_standby_streaming_delay = 3s`, on 15.20 to 18.7 (EE11a):

| Standby session | What happened |
|---|---|
| Idle in a `REPEATABLE READ` transaction that had read the table | Ended after the delay. The client's connection task received `FATAL 40001 terminating connection due to conflict with recovery` while idle; the next statement got `tokio-postgres`'s `connection closed`, with no database error attached |
| Idle in a `READ COMMITTED` transaction that had read the table | The same |
| Running a query on the table during replay | `ERROR 40001 canceling statement due to conflict with recovery` after 3.5 s; the connection stayed |
| Idle, not in a transaction, holding a prepared statement on the table | Untouched; its next run was right |
| Cached statements and functions (S1 to S5) | As on the primary with the reset: S1, S2 and S4 right, S3 `0A000`, S5 `22P02` retired, so the reset and the relabel replay; after a no-op `ALTER FUNCTION` on the primary replayed, S5 was right |
| The swap's replay | Waited 3.0 to 3.1 s behind the idle transactions |

The recovery-conflict routine is `ProcessInterrupts` on 15 and 16 and `ProcessRecoveryConflictInterrupt` on 17 and 18, so nothing reads it.

**SG, owner decision (EE11a):** a statement in a transaction that finds its connection closed by a `FATAL` `40001`, other than the `COMMIT`, returns `Error::Serialization`, which `.retry(n)` retries on a fresh connection; a loss found by the `COMMIT` stays `CommitUnknown`; a held connection gets `ConnectionLost` naming the conflict (R-1247, R-0697, T-0307, T-0305, T-0172, R-1252). CI gets a `standby` job in step 12, so steps 12, 20 and 59 can each test on it (R-1244); that placement is a choice of this fix, because step 59, which used to add the job, can run beside step 20.

### 18. `plpgsql_check`: pass, with an owner-approved change (SH)

`postgresql-NN-plpgsql-check` 2.10.16 installed from PGDG into the running `postgres:NN` service with `docker exec ... apt-get install`, with no restart (`pg_postmaster_start_time()` unchanged). A superuser made `morm_plpgsql_check` with the extension and `ALTER DATABASE ... IS_TEMPLATE true ALLOW_CONNECTIONS false`. Everything else ran as `p18_user`, with `CREATEDB` and not superuser: it cloned the template (`CREATE DATABASE ... TEMPLATE morm_plpgsql_check`), got `42501` from `CREATE EXTENSION plpgsql_check` in a database it made from `template0`, which holds no extension, and found the extension in the clone. Each check was `plpgsql_check_function_tb('<regprocedure>', fatal_errors => false, all_warnings => true)`, plus the trigger arguments. 66 checks per version, all passing, with identical findings on 15 to 18:

- **A column dropped from a branch a passing call misses:** the call returns 1, and the check reports `error 42703 column "gone" does not exist`.
- **False shapes, and the line that clears each** (R-1079's list). Each pragma'd function also ran in the database with no extension and returned its value, so to Postgres the line is only a string.

| Shape | Finding without a pragma | Cleared by |
|---|---|---|
| A temp table the function creates | `error 42P01 relation "tt" does not exist`, per statement | `PERFORM 'PRAGMA:TABLE: tt (a int4)';` after the `CREATE` |
| A table the function creates | `error 42P01 relation "made_here" does not exist` | `PERFORM 'PRAGMA:TABLE: made_here (a int4)';` |
| A temp table the caller creates | `error 42P01 relation "caller_tmp" does not exist` | `PERFORM 'PRAGMA:TABLE: caller_tmp (a int4)';` |
| A record filled by `EXECUTE` | `error 55000 record "r" is not assigned yet` | `PERFORM 'PRAGMA:TYPE: r (a int4)';` after the `EXECUTE` |
| A record with other fields by branch | `error 42703 record "r" has no field "a"` | `PERFORM 'PRAGMA:TYPE: r (a int4, b int4)';` after the branches, or `PRAGMA:DISABLE:CHECK` in a block around the reads |
| `set_config` of `search_path` | `error 42P01 relation "only_here" does not exist` | `PERFORM 'PRAGMA:TABLE: only_here (a int4)';`, or `PRAGMA:DISABLE:CHECK` in a block |
| A constant `1/0` | `error 22012 division by zero` | `PERFORM 'PRAGMA:DISABLE:CHECK';` in a block around it |

- **`PRAGMA:DISABLE:CHECK` hides a real break until its block ends:** with a column read inside the block (line 6) and again after it (line 10), only line 10 was reported.
- **A `PRAGMA:TABLE` line that no longer matches** (`t (id int4, a int4, gone int4)` after `gone` was dropped) hid the break: no `error`, while calling that path failed with `42703`.
- **Narrowing and widening:** an `int4` variable reading `int8` gives `performance 42804 target type is different type than source type`, detail `cast "bigint" value to "integer" type`, and nothing with default arguments; an `int8` variable reading `int4` gives the same with detail `cast "integer" value to "bigint" type`. `numeric(6,2)` reading `numeric(20,4)` gives no such finding; `varchar(3)` reading `text` gives `cast "text" value to "character varying" type` (an implicit cast in `pg_cast`, so not a narrowing by R-1080). Every function also gets a `performance 00000 routine is marked as VOLATILE` finding, which R-1080 ignores.
- **`EXECUTE`:** a constant string and a `format` of constants each report `error 42703` for the dropped column; `EXECUTE 'SELECT ' || c || ' FROM ex'` reports only a `security` finding, nothing about the column.
- **`plpgsql_check.mode = 'disabled'`** over a function with a real `42703`: set by `SET` (before or after the library loads), on the role, or in the connection's `options`, the check returns no findings and one `NOTICE 00000: plpgsql_check is disabled`, and `current_setting('plpgsql_check.mode', true)` reads `disabled` before the library loads; with nothing set it reads NULL, and with `by_function` the check runs. On the function, `ALTER FUNCTION ... SET plpgsql_check.mode = 'disabled'` by `p18_user` fails with `42501` `permission denied to set parameter` before the library loads in that session and works after it; then that function alone gets no findings and the `NOTICE`, `current_setting` reads NULL, and `pg_proc.proconfig` holds `plpgsql_check.mode=disabled`, while other functions are still checked. This is R-1082 and T-0204 as written.
- **Trigger forms**, with the table and transition tables read from `pg_trigger`: `BEFORE ROW`, `AFTER ROW`, `AFTER STATEMENT`, transition tables (with `oldtable` and `newtable`) and `INSTEAD OF` on a view each report `42703` for the dropped column. Without `oldtable` and `newtable`, a transition-table function, healthy or not, gets `42P01 relation "nt" does not exist`. A trigger function with no trigger, checked with no table, fails with `22023 missing trigger relation`. An event trigger function, a procedure and a polymorphic function need nothing more and report the `42703`. A `language sql` function fails with `22023 f_sql() is not a plpgsql function`.
- **`@plpgsql_check_options` can hide an `error`.** A trigger function on `tc` whose body holds `-- @plpgsql_check_options: relid = other`, where `other` still has the column `tc` lost, gets no findings when checked with `'tc'::regclass`: the comment's table wins over the argument. With `use_incomment_options => false` the `42703` comes back. `without_warnings = true` and `performance_warnings = false` in a comment also hide a narrowing's finding. So step 59 refuses functions containing `@plpgsql_check_options`, as the step file says; the option is `relid`, and the finding it hid was `error 42703 record "new" has no field "gone"`.
- **Pragmas that hide a narrowing (EE6).** The spike didn't test `disable:performance_warnings`. The design session did, with 2.10.16 on 15.20 to 18.7, on a function with `v int` reading a `bigint` column, checked with `all_warnings => true`:

| Line in the function | R-1080's finding |
|---|---|
| none | `performance 42804 cast "bigint" value to "integer" type` |
| `PERFORM 'PRAGMA:DISABLE:PERFORMANCE_WARNINGS';` | **gone** |
| the same in lower case | **gone** |
| `PERFORM plpgsql_check_pragma('disable:performance_warnings');` | **gone** |
| `aux int := plpgsql_check_pragma('disable:performance_warnings');` in `DECLARE` | **gone** |
| `PERFORM 'PRAGMA:DISABLE:CHECK';` in a block around the read | **gone** |
| `disable:other_warnings`, `extra_warnings`, `security_warnings` and `compatibility_warnings` | reported |
| `disable:performance_warnings` in an inner block that ends before the read | reported |
| `disable:performance_warnings` then `enable:performance_warnings` | reported |
| `'PRAGMA: disable : performance_warnings'`, with spaces | reported, with `WARNING unsupported pragma` |
| `PERFORM E'PRAGMA:DISABLE:PERFORMANCE\x5fWARNINGS';` | **gone**, and a word search of `prosrc` misses it (EF3) |
| `PERFORM U&'PRAGMA:DISABLE:PERFORMANCE\005FWARNINGS';` | **gone**, missed the same way |
| `PERFORM plpgsql_check_pragma(E'disable:performance\x5fwarnings');` | **gone**, missed the same way |
| `PERFORM plpgsql_check_pragma('disable:performance' \|\| '_warnings');` | reported: only a constant is read |
| `PERFORM E'PRAGMA:DISABLE:\x43HECK';` in a block around the read | **gone**, and a search for `disable:check` misses it |

The two `plpgsql_check_pragma(...)` forms fail at run time with `42883` in a database without the extension; the short forms are strings and run anywhere. The design session's rows ran locally; the second review found the escaped rows on 18.6, and `spikes.yml` then ran every row of this table on 15.20, 16.16, 17.12 and 18.7 with PGDG's `postgresql-NN-plpgsql-check` 2.10.16-1.pgdg13+2, each function also called and returning its value, with the same result on all four (run below).

**SH, owner decision:** `performance_warnings` anywhere in a checked function's source, in any case, stops `makemigrations` with exit code `2`; a function holding `disable:check` that's checked on a change to a column's type, length or precision gets its own line in the narrowing decision, whose default refuses (R-1093, R-1080, T-0310, R-1084). **EF3, owner decision:** before both searches, morm decodes every `E'...'` and `U&'...'` literal in the source by Postgres's rules and searches the decoded text too (R-1093, R-1080, T-0310).

### Owner decisions on step 55

Q1 to Q4 were the step session's questions; the independent review (`reviews/step-55-review.md`) found EE1 to EE11. The owner's answers, with where each is written in:

- **EA (Q1, EE2):** enum and composite result columns are read as `::text` (proof 13).
- **SA (EE1):** every migration `tx` step ends with the reset, and a retired enum type's labels are renamed `_morm_retired_<label>` (proofs 14 and 15).
- **SB (Q4, EE5):** a no-op `ALTER` recompiles unmanaged functions the migrating role owns, a `22P02` naming a retired type is a stale session, and R-1040's text says when to run `RECONNECT` (proof 15).
- **SC (Q2, EE3):** stale errors are recognised without translated text (proof 14).
- **SD, SE and SF (Q3, EE4, EE11b):** the shared statement generation behind PgBouncer, kept in the `_morm` schema's comment, raised only for a confirmed stale statement (proof 16).
- **SG (EE11a):** a standby's recovery conflict inside a transaction is `Serialization` (proof 17).
- **SH (EE6):** `performance_warnings` is refused, and `disable:check` gets a line in the narrowing decision (proof 18).
- **EE7 to EE9,** as the review recommends: R-0994 now names what `DISCARD PLANS` clears (proof 15), the statements cached before `ADD VALUE` are recorded and T-0148 runs them (proof 13), and the composite decoder's NULL fields and bounds are recorded, for the text form EA chose (proof 13).
- **EE10, the step's end-to-end check:** it asks for a pass or an owner-approved change for each item. Proofs 13 and 18 pass; proofs 14, 15, 16 and 17 each now have an owner-approved change written in, above, so the check is met.

- **The second review, EF1 to EF9** (`reviews/step-55-review-2.md`): EF1 and EF3 by the owner, as above; EF2, EF4 and EF5 as above; EF6, a loud README notice for R-1248's refusal and for the reset's re-planning of every session (R-1255); EF7, `import` leaves out retired types and columns and never advises renaming a retired type's labels (R-1001, R-1249); EF8, owner decision on the path, a rollback of an online enum swap builds a retired copy of a materialized view again on the restored type (R-1050, R-1062, T-0175), because the copy reads the retired column, which the swap's down doesn't bring back, so moving it back would leave a live view on the retired column and the retired type; EF9, both review files cited above, R-1083 and R-1244 naming the `standby` job and its primary service, and the evidence re-run in CI, below.

- **The third review, EG1 to EG8** (`reviews/step-55-review-3.md`):
  - **EG1 (owner decision):** inside a transaction, a stale `22P02` discards only that connection after the rollback and raises nothing; `.retry(n)`'s next attempt runs on a newly opened connection, and the generation rises only if that attempt runs the failed statement without the error (R-0984, T-0312, R-1040, R-1249, R-1250). The review showed on 18.6 that a function reading a stored `_morm_retired_x` into `v st` gives the same `22P02` from a call whose parameters hold no such text, which the earlier text would have answered by replacing the whole pool.
  - **EG2 (owner decision):** once a retry on a new connection fails for a statement, the pool remembers it, by a 64-bit hash of its text, for 60 seconds or until the connection generation moves, in at most 64 entries per pool, and returns its `22P02` at once meanwhile, whatever the client sent (R-0981, R-1245, T-0314). The review showed on 18.6 that `lower(p)` of `_MORM_RETIRED_X`, and `p->>'s'` of a `jsonb` holding `"_morm_retired_x"`, reach `enum_in` as `_morm_retired_x` while no parameter holds that text.
  - **EG3:** the new connection is opened outside the pool's slots but counted against the process's budget; on success it takes the first one's slot, on failure it's closed; with the budget full, morm closes the first and opens the new one in its slot; if it can't be opened, the original error is returned; the pool's size never changes and never passes `max` or the budget (R-0981, T-0311).
  - **EG4:** `SqlType` gains `READ_AS_LIST` beside `CAST_LIST`, so a generic `Vec<T>` takes `T::READ_AS_LIST` as its `READ_AS` on stable Rust (`contracts.md`, R-1241, R-1213).
  - **EG5 (owner decision):** a plain `'...'` literal is decoded as an `E` string too whenever `standard_conforming_strings` is off for the function, by its `SET` clause or the database's or role's default, and the `E` and `U&` prefixes are read in either case (R-1093, R-1080, T-0310, R-1084). This fix checked on 18.6 that a function declared `SET standard_conforming_strings = off` returning `'PRAGMA:DISABLE:\x43HECK'` returns `PRAGMA:DISABLE:CHECK` while `prosrc ~* 'disable:check'` is false.
  - **EG6 (owner decision):** EF8's rebuild runs in the rollback's one transaction; `rollback` opens the same contents decision a rebuild opens on the way up; the rollback's `_morm.history` row records the retired copy, column and type, so `purge` finds them; and a second run names its retired objects with a suffix holding its own `_morm.history` id (R-1050, R-1062, T-0175, R-1249). This fix ran the order on throwaway Postgres 17.12, built from ftp.postgresql.org's tarball checked by its published SHA-256, and 18.6: with a populated and an unpopulated materialized view above an enum column, in the state an online swap's cutover leaves, changing the live column under the live copy failed with `cannot alter type of a column used by a view or rule`, while one transaction that dropped the live copies, swapped the column to a restored type mapping the placeholders back, dropped the swap's type and created both copies again worked: the rebuilt copy read `a` and `b` as the restored type, the unpopulated one stayed unpopulated, and the retired copy kept its frozen rows, the deleted row 3 included, as `_morm_retired_<label>`. Renaming another type to the same retired name then failed with `type "_morm_retired_m2_st" already exists`, and a suffixed name worked; `purge`'s order, the copies, then the retired column, then the type, ran with no `CASCADE`. Both versions printed the same.
  - **EG7 and EG8:** as the review recommends: R-1062's sentence, the reason for EF8's path above, R-1238's `max_prepared_statements = 200` line, R-1083, R-1243's fix text, R-1254 and R-1084's notices, and this file's SD paragraph.

- **The fourth review, EH1 to EH8** (`reviews/step-55-review-4.md`):
  - **EH1 (owner decision):** a stale `22P02` in a transaction, on a connection opened after that statement's first such failure, counts as a failed confirmation, so the statement enters R-0981's memory, and a transaction with no `.retry(n)` stops costing a connection per call; behind a pooler a transaction discards nothing (R-0984, R-0981, T-0312, R-1040, R-1249, R-1250).
  - **EH2 (owner decision):** a transaction's confirming attempt confirms only with the same statement text and the same parameter bytes; anything else raises nothing, and the new connection stays as an ordinary one (R-0984, T-0312).
  - **EH3 (owner decision):** `up.sql` and `down.sql` name retired objects unsuffixed and stay byte for byte what the checksum covers; the ledger records each run's actual retired names, and `migrate` and `rollback` map them, as whole identifiers outside strings and comments, in the text they send (R-1050, R-0995, T-0175). This fix ran it on throwaway Postgres 17.12, built from ftp.postgresql.org's tarball checked by its published SHA-256, and 18.6, with the files held in a table, their SHA-256 taken first, and a function that maps each run's recorded names into the text with `regexp_replace` on whole words before `EXECUTE`: a migration that removes label `c`, mapping it to `b` and keeping old values, ran up and was rolled back; a row changed and a row inserted between the runs; the second run as written failed with `type "_morm_retired_m2_st" already exists`; with its names mapped to `_morm_retired_m2_st_7` and `_morm_retired_m2_s_7` it ran; rolling it back with the file as written ran with no error but read the first run's retired column and left rows 1 and 4 as `b`, while with its names mapped it restored `c`, `a`, `a` and `c`, each row from its own run's retired column; and both stored files still matched their checksums. Both versions printed the same.
  - **EH4:** the objects a rollback leaves are retired objects for `verify`, `check_schema`, the walk and R-1062's named refusal, not only `purge` (R-1050, R-0586, R-1062, T-0175).
  - **EH5:** `max` bounds the pool's slots, and the confirming connection is a momentary extra within the process's budget; a failed open in the full-budget branch leaves the slot to the pool's refill; a failed open fills the memory like a failed retry; in a transaction the attempt's connection takes the discarded one's slot (R-0981, R-0984, T-0311).
  - **EH6:** T-0314 shortens the cool-down through `morm`'s `#[doc(hidden)]` `test-hooks` feature, which only `morm-tests` enables, with a `repo_policy.rs` check, a choice of this fix the owner approves through PR #3 (R-0981, T-0314). Superseded by the tenth review's settings: T-0314 now sets `stale_cooldown = "1s"`, and the feature is gone.
  - **EH7:** plain literals are decoded only under the function's own `SET standard_conforming_strings = off`, because the check session sets the value `on`; T-0310's role-default row now expects the narrowing reported (R-1093, R-1080, T-0310, R-1084). The fourth review showed on 17.11 and 18.6 that a body with no `SET` clause, under a role default of `off`, isn't decoded in a session that sets the value `on`.
  - **EH8:** R-1249 says the rollback's decision stops the rollback with exit code `3` until `rollback --decide` answers it, and that a statement fed such text at a cutover can see its `22P02` for up to 60 seconds; R-0981 and R-1050 name this fix's own choices.

- **The fifth review, EI1 to EI9** (`reviews/step-55-review-5.md`). The owner decided that stale sessions heal by themselves, so the automatic retry on a fresh connection stays.
  - **EI1 (owner decision):** the record of a statement's first failure in a transaction also stores the database's statement generation, from the `_morm` schema's comment; a later failure on a newer connection counts as a failed confirmation only if that generation hasn't moved since, and otherwise as a new first failure; records are at most 64 per pool, oldest dropped, and cleared when the connection generation moves. So a second cutover's stale session heals as the first one's did (R-0984, R-0981, T-0312).
  - **EI2:** the name mapping rewrites identifier tokens, bare or quoted, matched on the decoded name and written back quoted, and never touches strings, comments or dollar quotes; a migration file naming a retired object inside one is refused (R-1050, new T-0315). This fix checked it on throwaway Postgres 17.12, built from ftp.postgresql.org's tarball checked by its published SHA-256, and 18.6, with files that quote every name and qualify it by schema or table as generated SQL does, mapped by a token-level mapper written for the check, not a regular expression. A migration removing label `c`, mapping it to `b` and keeping old values, ran up and was rolled back; row 1 changed and row 4 was inserted between the runs; the second run as written failed with `type "_morm_retired_m2_st" already exists`, and mapped it ran, six of its lines naming the suffixed names; its rollback as written ran with no error but left rows 1 and 4 as `b`, from the first run's retired column, while mapped it restored `c`, `a`, `a` and `c`; a file naming the retired column inside a dollar quote, a comment and a string was refused, naming each; a quoted, an upper-case bare, a `U&"..."` and a qualified name were mapped, while a longer name sharing the prefix and the name in a string, a comment and a dollar quote were left alone; and both stored files still matched their SHA-256. Both versions printed the same.
  - **EI3:** every command that runs migration text maps it: `migrate` and `--resume`, `rollback`, each pass of `rehearse`, and the forward migration `revert` writes, each to the names of the applied run, or the run being rolled back or resumed (R-1050, R-0583, R-0996, R-0618, T-0157).
  - **EI4:** inside and outside a transaction, a retry fails only on the same `22P02`; one that gets past the conversion confirms, whatever happens next, and the caller gets its result; a failed retry returns its `22P02` (R-0981, R-0984, T-0311).
  - **EI5:** a refused open filling the cool-down can delay a really stale session by up to 60 seconds; R-0981, R-1249, R-1250 and R-1040 now say so, and T-0311 asserts the call confirms once the cool-down ends with the limit lifted. This keeps EH5's rule and still heals.
  - **EI6:** a slot a failed open empties is left to the pool, which reopens on demand; T-0312 asserts the pool's size only where the attempt's connection takes the slot (R-0981, T-0311, T-0312).
  - **EI7:** `.retry(n)`'s attempt opens its connection through the pool's ordinary open, as part of that attempt; if it can't, `.retry(n)` returns that error and the statement enters the memory (R-0984, T-0312).
  - **EI8:** T-0175 asserts `check_schema` and the walk after a rollback.
  - **EI9:** R-1093 and R-0981 name all of this fix's choices, and the PR body lists them.

- **The sixth review, EJ1 to EJ9** (`reviews/step-55-review-6.md`):
  - **EJ1 (owner decision):** when Postgres refuses the confirming connection with `53300`, too many connections for the server or the role, morm closes the stale connection and opens the new one in its slot, as for a full budget, so the session heals while the limit lasts (R-0981, T-0311, R-1249). This fix confirmed on 18.6 that a role at its `CONNECTION LIMIT` is refused with `too many connections for role`, SQLSTATE `53300`.
  - **EJ2:** the first-failure record's time is taken when its generation is read, after the rollback, and a failure on a connection opened before that time keeps the record, so stored text costs at most the pool's older connections (R-0984, T-0312).
  - **EJ3:** T-0312's swaps raise the statement generation as a migration's `tx` step does, and a case with no `.retry(n)` reaches the generation comparison with a surviving record.
  - **EJ4:** R-1249 states the exit-`2` refusal of a retired name in a string, comment or dollar quote, and that a re-run's names get a `_<run>` suffix and the SQL morm sends is rewritten.
  - **EJ5:** behind a pooler morm keeps no first-failure record and treats such a `22P02` as `SchemaChanged`, so `.retry(n)` can reach another server session (R-0984, T-0312). **EJ5, owner decision:** a stale server session behind PgBouncer heals by itself. Where `[migrations] pgbouncer_admin_urls` lists PgBouncer admin consoles, `migrate`, `rollback` and `purge` send each one `RECONNECT` at the end of every migration, which replaces each server connection once it's released, interrupting nothing in flight; a failed send leaves the migration applied, is reported by host and port and exits with code `1`; a change made by hand sends nothing. Elsewhere the session heals within PgBouncer's `server_lifetime`, which the README suggests lowering. morm never ends a server session from the app (R-1256, R-1040, R-1250, R-1257, R-1258, T-0316). This fix ran it on throwaway PgBouncer 1.20.1 and 1.26.1, built from pgbouncer.org's tarballs checked against their published SHA-256 (`24992cf5…2088` and `00886a29…42d9`), in transaction mode with one server connection, in front of Postgres 18.6: a `DECLARE v st` function called through PgBouncer before a relabel swap gave a new client `22P02` naming the retired type on the same server session (the same backend pid); a transaction begun before `RECONNECT`, sleeping across it, ran its next statement on the same backend pid and committed; and the first client after it was released reached a new backend and got the right answer. Both versions printed the same. Postgres 15 to 17 weren't run here; T-0316 runs them.
  - **EJ6:** a retry ending in SQLSTATE class `08`, `40`, `53` or `57`, `55P03` or a lost connection neither confirms nor fails: it raises nothing, gets no slot and fills the cool-down (R-0981, R-0984, T-0311, T-0312).
  - **EJ7:** stored text in transactions costs a round of discards again after each migration, which R-0984, R-1249, R-1040 and R-1250 say.
  - **EJ8:** the mapping drops a `U&"..."` token's `UESCAPE` clause with it and scans `$` inside a bare identifier as Postgres does (R-1050, T-0315). This fix confirmed on 18.6 that `U&"!005fmorm_retired_m2_s" UESCAPE '!'` reads the column, that the quoted name written back without the clause reads the mapped one, that leaving the clause behind fails with `syntax error at or near "'!'"`, and that `x$_morm_retired_m2_s$` is one identifier.
  - **EJ9:** R-0984, R-0981 and R-1050 name the EI fix's own choices, and the PR body lists them.

- **The seventh review, EK1 to EK8** (`reviews/step-55-review-7.md`), which found the rule set sound straight to Postgres:
  - **EK1 (owner decision):** behind a pooler, a stale `22P02` in a transaction moves no marker, adds no salt and raises nothing, because new statement text runs in the same server session and calls the same compiled function; the statement enters the 60-second cool-down after its first `SchemaChanged`; healing behind PgBouncer stays with `RECONNECT` and `server_lifetime` (R-0984, R-1246, R-1249, R-1258, T-0312). The review found that with PgBouncer's default reuse, 20 of 20 transactions in a row reached the same server session on 1.20.1 and 1.26.1, so new text bought nothing.
  - **EK2:** with `so_reuseport`, several PgBouncer processes share an address and a connection reaches one; the review saw `RECONNECT` through the shared address succeed on 1.26.1 while the other process kept its stale session (3 of 8 new clients got `22P02`). morm reads `so_reuseport` from `SHOW CONFIG` after each `RECONNECT` over TCP and treats `1` as a failed send, and the README says to list each process's own Unix socket (R-1256, R-1258, T-0316).
  - **EK3:** a run that stops after committing a `tx` step, such as after a cutover, also sends `RECONNECT` before it exits (R-1256, R-1258, T-0316).
  - **EK4:** a retry confirms on any success, with or without rows (R-0981, R-0984, T-0311).
  - **EK5:** the admin console is driven with the simple query protocol only; the review saw it refuse the extended protocol on 1.20.1 and 1.26.1 (R-1256).
  - **EK6:** T-0316 runs on its own PgBouncer 1.20.1, 1.22 and 1.26.1 instances with one server connection, so it reaches no other test's sessions, and proves "nothing sent" with a released session keeping its backend pid (R-1238, T-0316).
  - **EK7 and EK8:** R-1256 names plain `RECONNECT` for every database and the rule's place in step 59 among this fix's choices; R-1258 states the `Error::Config` refusal and that a failed send needs `RECONNECT` by hand, because running `migrate` again sends nothing.

- **The eighth review, EL1 to EL7** (`reviews/step-55-review-8.md`), which found the retired-label path sound behind a pooler:
  - **EL1 (owner decision):** a `22P02` from a cached statement that casts a parameter to a non-built-in type, naming another enum, can come from a client's label: the review saw, on 18.6 straight and behind 1.26.1, a prepared `SELECT (r8.conv(($1::r8.kind)))::text` give `invalid input value for enum other: "b"` for `b`, a valid label of `kind`, and a prepared `UPDATE ... SET status = ($2::text)::r8.other WHERE email = ($1::public.citext)` the same for a client's `zzz`. So inside a transaction it's confirmed through `.retry(n)`'s next attempt, run unnamed behind a pooler or re-prepared straight; the marker moves and the generation rises only if that attempt succeeds with the same text and parameter bytes, and a repeat failure returns the error as it is (R-1245, R-0982, R-0984, R-1246, R-1253, T-0312).
  - **EL2 (owner decision):** behind a pooler, `42804` and `XX000` `cache lookup failed for type`, which also come from code compiled in the server session, move no marker, raise no generation and enter the 60-second cool-down, as EK1 does for the retired-label `22P02`; proof 16's table above showed new text doesn't cure them when they come from plpgsql (R-0984, R-0982, R-1246, R-1258, T-0312). Refined by EM1: proof 14's table records the same `XX000` from a plain cached statement, `SELECT ($1::st)`, after its type is dropped, which a re-prepare or `query_typed` cures, so that one is confirmed by an unnamed run first.
  - **EL3:** with `so_reuseport`, `migrate` reaches each PgBouncer process only through its own Unix socket, so only from that host; every TCP URL to such a PgBouncer is a failed send, which R-1258 and R-1256's message say, and T-0316 runs both branches against a real 1.26.1 run as two such processes.
  - **EL4:** the `RECONNECT` test's instances have a single-connection database, the one stated exception to EA's three-connection rule, for the "nothing sent" check, and a three-connection one on which T-0316 sees every server session replaced; all three set `max_prepared_statements = 0`, and morm's pools there turn the caches off (R-1238, T-0316).
  - **EL5:** T-0311's `UPDATE` case matches one row, so its `SET` list calls the function and reaches the retry; the review saw an `UPDATE` matching no row skip a `VOLATILE` function on 18.6.
  - **EL6 and EL7:** R-1257, R-1250 and R-1040 say "every PgBouncer process" and "when a run stops after a committed step"; R-1238 and R-1256 name this fix's choices, the TCP-only `so_reuseport` check among them.

- **The ninth review, EM1 to EM6** (`reviews/step-55-review-9.md`), which found the rules sound straight to Postgres:
  - **EM1 (owner decision, refining EL2):** behind a pooler, an `XX000` `cache lookup failed for type` from a cached statement is confirmed with one unnamed run, as EL1's `22P02` is: success means the cached text was stale, so the marker moves and every process heals; failure means plpgsql compiled in the server session, and EL2's no-marker, cool-down path follows. The review reproduced it behind 1.26.1 on 18.6 and 17.11: after a hand-run swap that dropped the old type, a cached statement and a new client's prepare of the same text both gave `XX000`, while the same text run unnamed, or text with a new marker, worked, and only the plpgsql `XX000` kept failing unnamed (R-1245, R-0718, R-0981, R-0982, R-0984, R-1246, R-1253, T-0306, T-0312).
  - **EM2:** the rules now say what the cool-down does for `42804` and a plpgsql `XX000` behind a pooler: their error returns at once, as `Error::Postgres`, with no retry, in a transaction or not (R-0981, R-0984, R-1246, T-0306, T-0312).
  - **EM3 (owner decision):** the pending confirmation of EL1's `22P02` and EM1's `XX000` is kept in the pool, by a hash of the text and parameter bytes, at most 64 with the oldest dropped, cleared when the statement or connection generation moves, so the statement's next run with the same bytes confirms, by `.retry(n)`, the app's own retry or a later call. The review saw, on 18.6 and 17.11 behind PgBouncer, three transactions in a row preparing the same text again each get the stale `22P02`, and new text in a fourth work (R-1246, R-0984, R-1253, T-0312). Refined by EN2 and EN5: the entry's whole lifecycle is now in R-1246, it expires after `stale_pending_expiry`, and it's cleared when the pool's statement generation, `<n>` or the connection generation moves.
  - **EM4:** T-0312 tests EL1's same-parameter-bytes gate with a closure that binds another value on its second attempt.
  - **EM5 and EM6:** R-0984 and R-1238 name this fix's choices (the straight re-prepare, the three-connection database); R-1258 says "every PgBouncer process's admin console".

- **The tenth review, EN1 to EN5** (`reviews/step-55-review-10.md`), nothing blocking:
  - **EN1 (owner decision):** behind a pooler, while a confirmation is pending for a statement, its runs with other parameter values go unnamed, so they get the right answer at once, even with an old type kept, until it confirms or the pending check expires; they confirm nothing (R-1246, R-0984, R-1253, R-1258, T-0312). The review saw, behind 1.26.1 on 18.6 and 17.11, a stale cached statement fail for every value while unnamed runs were right, and a shared-label `a < c` answer `true` cached and `false` unnamed.
  - **EN2:** the pending confirmation's lifecycle is defined once, in R-1246: at most `stale_pending_max`, oldest dropped; expiry after `stale_pending_expiry`; all cleared when the pool's statement generation moves, a confirmation's salt included, or `<n>` or the connection generation moves; the next run with the same parameter bytes takes the entry out, so only one run confirms; it confirms on success or on another server error outside R-0981's list, fails on the same error, and neither on an error on that list or a lost connection (R-1246, R-0984, T-0312). The review saw the dropped-type statement given `c` end unnamed in `22P02` `invalid input value for enum st: "c"`, which this rule counts as a confirmation.
  - **EN3:** while nothing is pending the check costs one relaxed atomic read per run; the text hash is compared only when the count isn't zero, and parameter bytes hashed only when the text matches (R-1246).
  - **EN4:** outside a transaction a plpgsql `XX000` whose pending run fails still gets R-0981's retry on another connection (R-1246, T-0306).
  - **EN5 (owner decision):** pending checks expire after 60 seconds by default.
  - **The owner's settings:** every number of the stale-session rules is a pool setting with today's value as its default, a `MORM_POOL_*` override and a "use this when" line: `stale_cooldown = "60s"` (at least `1s`), `stale_cooldown_max = 64` (at least 1), `stale_pending_expiry = "60s"` (`0` turns pending checks off), `stale_pending_max = 64` (`0` turns them off) and `stale_first_failure_max = 64` (at least 1). Values that would let text churn connections are refused with `Error::Config`; the two that turn pending checks off get a loud notice (R-0060, R-1259 to R-1262, R-1263 to R-1268, R-0981, R-0984, R-1246, T-0314, T-0317).

- **The eleventh review, EO1 to EO10** (`reviews/step-55-review-11.md`):
  - **EO1 and EO2 (owner decisions):** a confirming run proves a stale statement only if nothing else differs between the failed run and it. Behind PgBouncer the two can reach different server sessions: the review saw, after a type was dropped by hand, session A give `XX000` for a function prepared and unnamed while session B, which hadn't compiled it, answered unnamed. And a client can make the same bytes fail and then succeed by changing its own row: `UPDATE t SET o = conv($2) WHERE id = $1 AND k = $3` with `$3::kind` gave `22P02` for `(1,'zzz','a')`, and `UPDATE 0` once the row's `k` was `'b'`. So a pool now allows at most one confirmation-driven cache clear, a salt or a statement-generation raise, per `stale_cooldown` while `<n>` is unmoved, and a stale signal from text prepared since that clear is treated as from compiled code, straight or behind a pooler; a `0A000` and a migration aren't limited (R-0982, R-1246, R-0718, R-1245, R-0984, R-1253, R-1258, T-0306, T-0312).
  - **EO3 and EO4 (owner decisions):** `stale_cooldown_max` and `stale_first_failure_max` keep a floor of `1`, with a loud line on what values below the number of such statements cost; every setting has a ceiling, `10m` for durations and `4096` for counts, refused above it with `Error::Config` naming the limit, and the README states each setting's cost (R-1262, R-1268, R-1263 to R-1267, R-0060, T-0317).
  - **EO5:** with pending checks off, behind a pooler a transaction's signal returns `SchemaChanged` on every call and moves nothing; straight to Postgres each connection evicts its own copy, so it heals as each fails once (R-1246, R-1262, R-1268, T-0312).
  - **EO6:** T-0312 tests `stale_pending_max = 2`, `stale_first_failure_max = 2` and EN5's no-salt-after-expiry.
  - **EO7:** an other-values unnamed run that ends in a plpgsql signal counts as that statement's failed confirmation; a client's bad label runs that statement unnamed for every caller for up to `stale_pending_expiry`, which R-1246 and R-1253 say (T-0312).
  - **EO8:** step 48 measures the pending check (R-1269).
  - **EO9 and EO10:** the fixed numbers left in R-0984, R-1040, R-1249, R-1250 and R-1253 now name their settings; R-0984 follows EN4; R-1262 says "every tunable"; `stale_pending_expiry` takes `"off"` like other pool durations, and `stale_cooldown = "off"` is refused.

- **The twelfth review, EP1 to EP8** (`reviews/step-55-review-12.md`), **and the consolidated stale-session design, MA to ME** (`reviews/stale-session-state-machine.md`, on `main`). After twelve rounds each found new gaps in the stale-session rules, the owner chose to replace them with one state machine; EP1 to EP8 are closed inside it. The owner took every recommendation:
  - **MA (owner decision):** straight to Postgres, one epoch per pool decides which connections a stale-session failure may replace: a connection opened before the epoch's start is replaced on its first such failure, one opened after returns its error as it is, and the replacement keeps the old connection's slot whatever its run returns. It replaces the per-statement cool-down memory straight to Postgres and the first-failure records, so `stale_first_failure_max` and `MORM_POOL_STALE_FIRST_FAILURE_MAX` go, and `stale_cooldown_max` applies behind a pooler only (R-0981, R-0984, R-1261, R-1262, R-1267, T-0311, T-0314, T-0317).
  - **MB (owner decision, refining EO1):** one gate for every pool-wide action, clearing every connection's caches, adding a salt and replacing every connection, `0A000`, `42804` and the connection generation included; at most one per `stale_cooldown` plus one per migration; a request inside the window is taken at the first checkout after it ends; the window never changes a call's own outcome (R-0982, R-0975, R-1270, T-0318).
  - **MC (owner decision):** `stale_pending_expiry = 0` and `"0"` are bare numbers, refused; `"off"` and `"0s"` turn pending entries off (R-1262, R-1268, T-0317).
  - **MD (owner decision):** a `search_path` set per transaction over tables of different shapes is handled by the machine at the stated cost, with a loud README notice beside `tx.set_local` (R-1272).
  - **ME (owner decision):** the randomised simulation uses `proptest` as a dev-dependency of `morm`, for shrinking a failing sequence (T-0319).
  - **The settings template (owner decision):** a commented `morm.example.toml` listing every setting morm reads, with its default, allowed values, environment variable and when to change it, kept in step with the plan by a test (R-1273, T-0320, R-1274, T-0321). **Superseded by the owner's decision on the thirteenth review:** a real `morm.toml`, not a template, below.
  - **Unknown `morm.toml` keys (owner decision, on this rewrite's open question):** config loading refuses every key it doesn't read, in every table, with `Error::Config` naming the key and its table; `timezone` and `stale_first_failure_max` keep their own messages; a loud README notice says so and points to the template; and every step that adds a setting adds it to the template, as steps 39, 51, 52, 59 and 60 now say (R-1275, T-0322, R-1276, R-1273, T-0320, R-1261).
  - The machine's state, its signals and its table are written once, in step 14 (R-1245, R-1270, R-0718, R-0981, R-0982, R-1246, R-1262), with the rows inside a transaction built in step 20 (R-0984) and the guarantees there too (R-1271). Every earlier owner decision stands as the design's ledger marks it. The mechanisms of these earlier fixes change with it, as the design's "Fix choices that change" lists: EG3's failed retry closing the new connection, EI1's and EG2's records and straight-to-Postgres memory, EN1's "behind a pooler only", EM3's key by text and parameter bytes, EO1's window leaving `0A000` out, and EO10's `0` meaning off.

- **The thirteenth review, EQ1 to EQ12** (`reviews/step-55-review-13.md`), which found the machine written in faithfully, and three owner decisions:
  - **A real `morm.toml` (owner decision, replacing the template):** `crates/morm-core/morm.toml` ships in `morm-core`'s package with every setting present and set to its default, each with what it does, its allowed values, its environment variable and when to change it, and loud `# WARNING:` lines beside values that turn a protection off; loading it unchanged gives exactly the defaults. The README links to it and says to copy it next to the app, and `morm-admin init` writes it as a new project's `morm.toml` (R-1273, R-1274, R-1276, T-0320, T-0321, step 42's deliverables).
  - **EQ1:** a connection field a URL can also carry (`host`, `port`, `name`, `user`, `password`, `sslmode`, the certificate paths, `target_session_attrs`, `connect_timeout`, `application_name`, and `[database.migration]`'s `user` and `password`) is a `${VAR}` placeholder naming its own variable, with its default and R-0060's example in the comment, so a copied file never overrides `DATABASE_URL`'s parts; T-0320 loads the file with `DATABASE_URL` set to another host and database and gets the URL's parts (R-1273, T-0320). Settings with no default are placeholders too, and the per-pool tables are commented-out examples of the shape.
  - **`sslmode` (owner decision, on EQ1's open part):** `sslmode` defaults to `require`: TCP connections use TLS, and a server without it is refused. It applies to TCP only; a Unix socket never negotiates TLS whatever it says, so local socket use works unchanged, and a server reached over TCP without TLS needs `sslmode = "disable"` set explicitly, with a loud README notice (R-1277, R-0111, R-0060, T-0323, R-1278, R-1279). The evidence is "TLS over a Unix socket" below: `tokio-postgres` 0.7.18 fails `require` on a socket, so morm gives the driver `SslMode::Disable` for every socket host.
  - **EQ2 (owner decision):** while a statement has a pending entry, every run of it is unnamed straight to Postgres too, as behind PgBouncer, so a per-transaction `search_path` costs the same in both modes: at most one failed transaction per statement per `stale_pending_expiry`, plus one after each pool-wide action, which removes every entry, and unnamed runs while it's pending. The entry's horizon and the per-statement prepare sequence go. R-1272's loud notice gives the mitigations: name the schema in the SQL or build it with fragments, keep every tenant's tables the same shape, set `search_path` per pool, use `.retry(n)`, or tune `stale_pending_expiry` or turn the statement cache off for that pool (R-0718, R-1270, R-1271, R-0984, R-1272, R-1268, R-1253, R-1269, T-0312, T-0318, T-0319).
  - **EQ3:** `SchemaChanged` is returned when the next attempt may succeed: after a repair, on D2's first time behind PgBouncer, on the transient `XX000`, and on D1 with pending entries off; otherwise `Error::Postgres` (`contracts.md`, R-0984).
  - **EQ4:** outside a transaction behind PgBouncer a moved `<n>` is adopted, never salted, and adopting sets the window's `<n>` and starts a window, so one migration lets one pool-wide action through (R-1270, R-0982, T-0318).
  - **EQ5:** R-1271's churn and T-0319's I4 allow `2 × max + 2` stale-driven opens per `stale_cooldown`, the `+ 2` being an epoch's two failed opens, plus `2 × max` per migration; I9 is split as R-1271's silent wrong answers are.
  - **EQ6:** T-0319 adds a fault switch for each of the design's seven broken machines, each of which must be caught within the CI case count by the invariant the design names.
  - **EQ7:** straight to Postgres, `<n>` is read only for the old-connection test and a closed-window request, and a read finding it moved makes no entry; R-1270's procedure has row A4's branch; R-1245 counts a session-class `XX000` as certain (R-1270, R-1245, R-0718, T-0318, T-0319).
  - **EQ8:** the `sslmode` warning names a real loud notice, R-1278. **EQ9:** the loader keeps one table of settings that its validation reads, and T-0320 compares each comment's range with it. **EQ10:** R-1263 names the window, the epoch and the cool-down. **EQ11:** T-0322 tests `morm-migrate`, and R-1275 is the one place that says the removed-setting message applies in any table. **EQ12:** the PR body's stale sentence is marked superseded.

- **The fourteenth review, ER1 to ER7** (`reviews/step-55-review-14.md`), which found EQ1 to EQ12 and the three decisions written in, and one owner decision:
  - **ER1:** straight to Postgres the pool reads `<n>` when it starts too, as behind PgBouncer, through R-1116's startup statement, so its last-read `<n>` is always set; every read that finds `<n>` moved calls `on_moved`, the gate's own read included; and only the call whose read found it moved skips its pending entry. T-0318, T-0319's unit test of rows B and D and T-0312's `search_path` case with a migration assert it (R-1270, R-0982, R-1116, T-0318, T-0319, T-0312).
  - **ER2 (owner decision, option (a)):** `morm-admin init` keeps writing a found socket's `host` and `port` as active lines, but loudly: only the lines whose found value isn't the default, a non-default port in the default directory included, each with a `# WARNING:` line saying it overrides `DATABASE_URL` and how to undo it; a loud README notice; and a startup warning whenever a value written in `[database]` or `[database.migration]` overrides a different one in its URL, naming both and never a password. R-0062 now says the separate fields override a URL's parts wherever each is set. R-0107's TCP alternative carries `sslmode = "disable"` with its warning (R-1280, R-1282, R-1283, R-1284, R-0062, R-0107, T-0324, T-0325).
  - **ER3:** R-1272's notice and R-1271 say the switches request repairs themselves, so with recurring values or several such statements failures come about once per `stale_cooldown`, and the advice is to raise `stale_pending_expiry` and `stale_cooldown` together, with what a longer `stale_cooldown` costs (T-0312). **Refined by the owner's `0A000` decision below:** a confirming run no longer requests after a `0A000`, so values that recur ask for nothing, and only several such statements set the rate by `stale_cooldown`.
  - **ER4:** `42804` and a session-class `XX000` on an old connection request *replace* once, at the failure, and the confirmation after asks nothing more (R-1245, R-1270, R-0981's rows A2 and B3, T-0318, T-0319).
  - **ER5:** R-1277 names every connection morm opens, `scratch_url`, the PgBouncer admin URLs and `morm-admin`'s own among them; R-1258 says what an admin URL to a PgBouncer without client TLS needs; `init`'s CI workflow sets `sslmode = disable` loudly (R-1281); T-0323 adds a mixed host list without `tls`, T-0316 an admin URL with `sslmode` unset; R-1278 says how to turn on the `tls` feature. The evidence is below, "PgBouncer's admin console without client TLS".
  - **ER6:** R-1273 lists the R-0060 values that aren't defaults, T-0320 fails on any other default that isn't R-0060's value, and R-0102 gives the default `port`, `5432`.
  - **ER7:** the PR body's two history lines on the superseded straight-to-Postgres mechanism are marked superseded.
  - **`morm-admin` always carries TLS (owner decision, on the fix round's first open question):** `morm-admin` always builds with rustls, with no feature that leaves it out, and the release binaries include it, so `sslmode`'s default, `require`, works for every TCP connection it opens, and step 10's refusal for want of the `tls` feature never comes from it (R-1285, T-0326 and the deliverable line in step 33, R-1277, R-1278, `contracts.md`'s R-0019 and crate table).
  - **A `0A000` requests a repair once (owner decision, on the fix round's second open question):** inside a transaction a `0A000` requests *clear*, or *salt* behind PgBouncer, once, at the failure, as `42804` and `XX000` do; the pending entry it makes is marked, and its confirming run requests nothing more, while a later signal that isn't a `0A000` unmarks it. So one statement under a per-transaction `search_path` fails about once per `stale_pending_expiry` again, its values recurring or not, once more per `stale_cooldown` when `stale_pending_expiry` is the shorter, and several such statements about once per `stale_cooldown` each. R-1272's advice now says to raise `stale_pending_expiry`, keeping it at least `stale_cooldown`, and to raise both together when several statements switch (R-0718, R-1270, R-1245, R-1271, R-1272, T-0312, T-0318, T-0319). T-0312's bounds come from the procedure as written, checked against a small model of the gate, the window and the entries with switches every 100 ms: at `stale_pending_expiry = "10s"` and `stale_cooldown = "1s"`, two statements fail five times each in five seconds, inside the test's three to six, and one statement repeating its `id` fails once, against five before the decision; at `"1s"` and `"3s"` it fails six times in six seconds, inside four to eight. Neither decision rests on how Postgres behaves, so nothing new ran on it.
  - **Raised by this round, for the owner:** whether `morm-migrate`, used as a library without `morm-admin`, carries TLS, and which `morm` features the `morm-mgr` package `init` creates turns on, since its data hooks connect through `morm`. Both are open questions in PR #3's body. **Both answered by the owner, option (a) each, below.**
  - **`morm-migrate` always carries TLS (owner decision, on the first of those questions):** `morm-migrate`, used on its own as a library too, always builds with rustls from step 29, where it first connects, with no feature that leaves it out, as `morm-admin` does, so `sslmode`'s default, `require`, works for every TCP connection it opens, whoever calls it, and step 10's refusal for want of the `tls` feature never comes from it; `morm-admin`'s connections through it use its connector (R-1286, T-0327 and the deliverable line in step 29, R-1277, R-1278, `contracts.md`'s R-0019 and crate table).
  - **`morm-mgr` turns on `tls` (owner decision, on the second):** `morm-admin init` always writes the `morm` dependency of the `morm-mgr` package it creates with `features = ["tls"]`, under a comment saying why and naming the README notice on `sslmode`, so a data hook against a TCP database with `sslmode` set nowhere connects with TLS instead of stopping mid-migration at a `no-tx` step (R-1287, T-0328 and the deliverable line in step 42, R-1277, R-1278, `contracts.md`'s R-0019 and crate table). Evidence, from a throwaway Cargo workspace under `.claude/scratch/` with `TMPDIR` there, built offline with Cargo 1.99.1 and resolver 3: a stand-in `morm` with a `tls` feature, an app depending on it without `tls`, and a `morm-mgr` depending on both with `features = ["tls"]` on `morm`. `morm`'s `tls` stayed on in `morm-mgr`'s build, by `cargo tree -e features` and by `cfg!(feature = "tls")` at run time, with no flags, `--no-default-features`, `--all-features` and `--features` naming one of its features, so `check-sql`'s pass-through flags can't take it off. With `morm-mgr` a member of the app's workspace, `cargo build -p app` left the app's `morm` without `tls`, but `cargo build --workspace` and a plain `cargo build` at the virtual workspace's root turned it on for the app's own binary too; with `morm-mgr` its own workspace, excluded from the app's, `cargo build --workspace` left the app without it, `morm-mgr` kept it, and `morm-mgr` got a `Cargo.lock` of its own. The plan doesn't say which layout `init` writes, so that's raised below. Neither decision rests on how Postgres behaves, so nothing ran on it; T-0327 and T-0328 run where they're built. The probe was deleted.
  - **Raised by this round, for the owner:** whether the `morm-mgr` package `init` creates is a member of the app's workspace, since there a workspace build turns `tls` on for the app's own `morm` too, as above. An open question in PR #3's body. **Answered by the owner, option (a), below.**
  - **`morm-mgr` is a member of the app's workspace (owner decision, on that question):** the `morm-mgr` package `init` creates is a member of the project's Cargo workspace, listed in the root `Cargo.toml`'s `[workspace] members` beside the app's package, with no `[workspace]` of its own and no `exclude` naming it, so the project has one `Cargo.lock`, at its root. Because its `morm` has `tls`, Cargo's feature unification turns `tls` on for the app's own `morm` in a build that includes `morm-mgr` and not in a build of the app alone, so an app without `tls` that reaches its database over TCP can start in CI or dev and stop at startup in a deploy build, with step 10's `Error::Config` naming `sslmode` and the `tls` feature (R-0111). R-1287 says `init` makes `morm-mgr` a member and that its comment says which builds turn `tls` on for the app and what such an app does: turn `tls` on in its own `morm` dependency, or set `sslmode = "disable"` only over a network it trusts, and check a build of the app alone with `cargo tree -p <app> -e features`; R-1278's notice says the same to the reader; T-0328 checks both builds, by the `features` of `morm`'s `compiler-artifact` message in `--message-format=json`, and the one `Cargo.lock`; step 42's deliverable line and `contracts.md`'s R-0019 and `morm-mgr` crate row say so. Evidence, from a throwaway Cargo workspace under `.claude/scratch/` with `TMPDIR` there, built offline with Cargo 1.99.1 and resolver 3, a stand-in `morm` whose `tls` feature sets a constant the app's binary prints, and a `morm-mgr` depending on the app and on `morm` with `features = ["tls"]`, in two layouts. With a virtual workspace root and the app in its own folder, the app's binary had `tls` after a plain `cargo build` at the root, `cargo build --workspace`, `cargo build --release --workspace`, and `cargo build --workspace` run in the app's folder, and didn't after `cargo build -p app`, `cargo build --release -p app`, `cargo run -p app`, a plain `cargo build` in the app's folder, and `cargo install --path app`. With the app's own package at the root and `morm-mgr` its member, the app had `tls` after `cargo build --workspace` only: a plain `cargo build` at the root, `cargo build -p app` and `cargo install --path .` left it off. In each build `morm`'s `compiler-artifact` message listed `["tls"]` exactly when the app's binary printed it on, and each layout had one `Cargo.lock`, at its root. So a plain build at the root turns `tls` on for the app only when the root is a virtual workspace; the plan doesn't fix which layout `init` writes, so R-1287, R-1278 and T-0328 are worded to hold for both, and T-0328 checks the one `init` writes. Nothing here rests on how Postgres behaves, so nothing ran on it. The probe was deleted.

- **The fifteenth review, ES1 to ES5** (`reviews/step-55-review-15.md`), which found ER1 to ER7 and the five owner answers written in, nothing blocking and no owner decision needed. Each is fixed as the review recommends:
  - **ES1:** row B2's confirming run, after its old connection was discarded, requests *replace* in place of the entry's *clear*, never beside it. `expect_confirm` sets a *replace* bit on the statement's pending entry, which a later signal's refresh clears and row B2 sets again when it discards; with no entry, because the read found `<n>` moved or entries are off, and in row B3, it marks the transaction's next attempt, whose run of the same text and bytes requests *replace* if it confirms. R-1245's "one failure never asks twice" now names row B2. T-0319's unit test of rows B and D asserts one *replace* and nothing due on an old connection, and one *clear* on one that isn't old; T-0312's B2 asserts no *clear* beside it (R-1270, R-0718, R-1245, T-0319, T-0312).
  - **ES2:** every pool reads `<n>` when it starts, the statement caches on or off, through R-1116's statement or step 14's own query with `check_schema = "off"`, and the marker stays behind a pooler with the caches on; "with the caches off no call reaches the gate unread" is gone, and `expect_confirm`'s request uses the pool's last-read `<n>`, on an entry and on the next attempt's mark alike, with no read of its own. **Refined by ET1 below:** at `7ed642b` the mark kept the `<n>` its failure read, which could be older than the window's by the next attempt; now `request` judges every known `<n>` as the last-read one and the mark carries none. T-0319's settings add the caches off, in both modes, with a row B3 case; T-0318 asserts the start read with the caches on and off (R-1270, R-1116, T-0318, T-0319).
  - **ES3:** with several statements under a per-transaction `search_path`, each fails about once per `stale_cooldown`, or once per `stale_pending_expiry` when that's the shorter, because its entry expires before the due action ends it; raising `stale_pending_expiry` alone helps only up to `stale_cooldown`, so the advice to raise both together stands (R-1271, R-1272). The `0A000` decision's bullet above, "several such statements about once per `stale_cooldown` each", holds with `stale_pending_expiry` at least `stale_cooldown`. Checked against the same small model of the gate, the window and the entries, switches every 100 ms: at `stale_cooldown = "3s"` and `stale_pending_expiry = "1s"`, two statements fail 30 times each in 30 seconds, once per `stale_pending_expiry`, with 10 actions; at `"1s"` and `"3s"` 30 times each, once per `stale_cooldown`; at `"3s"` and `"3s"` 10 times each. The model's earlier figures are unchanged: five each for two statements at `"10s"`/`"1s"`, one for one statement repeating its `id`, six for one statement at `"1s"`/`"3s"`. T-0312's bounds stand. Nothing here rests on how Postgres behaves, so nothing ran on it.

R-1282 and R-1280 name the new title; T-0325 adds `sslmode = "disable"` against `?sslmode=require` (R-1283, R-1282, R-1280, T-0325).

- **The sixteenth review, ET1** (`reviews/step-55-review-16.md`), which found ES1 to ES5 fixed as recommended and one finding, needing no owner decision, fixed as the review recommends:
  - **ET1:** a request is judged by the pool's last-read `<n>`, never an older one its call carried. `request` begins by counting a known `<n>` as the last-read one, because every read that finds `<n>` moved has called `on_moved`, so a `<n>` a call carries equals it or lags it. At `7ed642b` the gate took a request whose `<n>` merely differed from the window's, so a `<n>` kept across `.retry(n)`'s backoff by row B3's mark, or by row B2's with no entry, or read in row A2 before the new connection's run, passed a closed window after another call's action under a newer `<n>`, and set the window's `<n>` back, so the next request passed too. `expect_confirm`'s mark now carries no `<n>`: its request, like the entry's, uses the last-read one and makes no read. R-0982's request bullet and its bound say the `<n>` judged is the last-read one. T-0318 adds a request carrying an older `<n>` inside a window, and row A2's case; T-0319's unit test of rows B and D adds row B3's trace and row B2's with entries off, and its fault switches an eighth, a request judged by the `<n>` its call carried, which I1 must catch (R-1270, R-0982, T-0318, T-0319).

ET1's trace, row B3's `22P02` at 0 s reading 1, a migration at 0.05 s, another call's `42804` at 0.06 s reading 2 and taking *replace*, B3's next attempt confirming at 0.1 s and an unread request at 0.2 s: as written, three actions, *replace* under 2 at 0.06 s, *replace* under 1 at 0.1 s and *clear* under 2 at 0.2 s, two breaking I1; with the fix, one action at 0.06 s, B3's *replace* due and taken by the first checkout after the window, at 1.1 s, and none breaking I1. Row B2 with entries off gave the same, and row A2, with the migration and the other call's action during the new connection's run, gave three actions as written and one plus the due one with the fix. A request carrying `<n>` = 1 inside a window under 2 was taken as written, setting the window's `<n>` to 1, and is due with the fix, the window's `<n>` staying 2. Nothing here rests on how Postgres behaves, so nothing ran on it; the model was deleted.
  - **Raised by this round, for the owner:** reads of `<n>` that overlap. The procedure applies each read at once, but a read is a catalog query, so a read taken before a migration commits can reach the machine after one taken after it; `moved` then holds for the older `<n>`, `on_moved` sets the last-read `<n>` back, and behind PgBouncer the marker too. In the same model, with one migration, a call reading 2 and taking *replace*, then another call's read of 1 arriving after it, the fix above still gives three actions, two breaking I1, because the last-read `<n>` itself went back. Applying a read only when its `<n>` is greater, or ordering reads by when they were sent, gives one, but they differ on a lowered `<n>`, from a restore or a failover, which only the second adopts. An open question in PR #3's body, with options. **Answered by the owner, option (c), below.**
  - **Overlapping reads of `<n>` are ordered by ticket (owner decision, option (c), on that question):** every read of `<n>` takes a ticket from the pool's counter when it's sent, one atomic add, and R-1270's new `arrive` applies its result as one step, with nothing waiting. A read sent after the last applied read arrived, its ticket at or above the horizon, the counter's value at that arrival, counts as it is, lowered or not, so a lowered `<n>` after a restore, a failover to a replica that lost the last migration, a recreated database or a comment set by hand is adopted; a read that overlapped the last applied one counts only if its `<n>` is greater, and otherwise counts as the last-read `<n>`. Every applied read sets the horizon, moved or not. `read_n` now returns the `<n>` a read counts as and whether it moved, having called `on_moved`, in place of `moved(n)` at its four call sites, and everything that consumes a read takes that `<n>`: `on_moved`, the marker behind PgBouncer (R-1246), R-0981's epoch test, whose `T` is now the moment the read arrives, and R-0982's gate, its event E1 and its bound, which counts a lowered `<n>` as a migration. R-1271's churn bounds and memory follow. T-0318 adds overlapping reads applied out of order, a lowered `<n>` adopted from a read that doesn't overlap and refused from one that does, an overlapping greater `<n>`, a read that overlapped an unmoved applied read, the start read and a failed read; T-0319's model sends and applies reads apart, with snapshots anywhere between and lowerings while reads are in flight and while none is, adds I10, the read order, and four fault switches, one per broken order, and its unit test of rows B and D adds overlapping reads in rows B2, B3 and D1 and a lowered `<n>` in row D1, adopted and refused (R-1270, R-0981, R-0982, R-1246, R-1271, T-0318, T-0319).
  - **Checked against a small model** of R-1270's `read_n`, `arrive`, `on_moved`, `request` and `take`, in both modes, with reads sent, snapshotted and applied apart, run with the procedure as it stood at `9805559`, applying each read as it arrives, and as written now, with `stale_cooldown = "1s"`. The traces: the open question's, a read sent at 0 s seeing `<n>` = 1, a migration at 0.05 s, a read sent at 0.06 s seeing 2 and arriving at 0.07 s, the first arriving at 0.08 s and an unread request at 0.2 s, gave three actions at `9805559`, at 0.07, 0.08 and 0.2 s, with the last-read `<n>` going 1, 2, 1, 2, and one now, at 0.07 s, with the request due and taken by the first checkout after the window, behind PgBouncer as straight to Postgres; a lowered `<n>`, 5 to 3, read by a read that overlaps none, is adopted, as at `9805559`; the same lowered `<n>` found by a read sent before, and arriving after, a read that found 5 is refused and adopted by the next read. Random sequences, 256 of 2,000 events each, a read's latency 1 ms to 200 ms, migrations and lowerings at random, checking I1 as T-0319 now states it and I10: as written now, no sequence fails either, with migrations only, with lowerings while no read is in flight, and with lowerings while reads are, given T-0319's span after such a lowering; at `9805559`, all 256 fail I1 and I10 in each. Each new fault switch is caught: reads applied as they arrive in 256 of 256, by I1 and I10; reads ordered by when they were sent in 110, by I1 and I10; `<n>` that only rises in 256, by I10, once lowerings occur; and the horizon set only by a moving read in 11 with lowerings while no read is in flight and 45 with lowerings while reads are, by I10. Nothing here rests on how Postgres or PgBouncer behaves, so nothing ran on them; the model was deleted.
  - **Raised by this round, for the owner:** two cases the ticket order leaves outside, now stated in R-1270, R-0982, R-1271 and T-0319. First, a lowering while reads of `<n>` are in flight: a read from before the lowering that overlapped the last applied one counts if its `<n>` is greater, so after a lowered `<n>` is adopted, or found by a read that rose from an older `<n>`, it can bring back a `<n>` the database no longer holds, and the next read sets the lowered one again. In the model, a read seeing 5, a lowering to 3, a read seeing 3 applied first, then the first arriving, gave three actions in 0.21 s with one lowering, in both modes, and without T-0319's span every random sequence with lowerings while reads were in flight failed I1; a read that overlapped the last applied one never counting gave one action and no failing sequence anywhere, I1 checked with no span, and a third word, the counter's value when a lowered `<n>` was last adopted, left 4 of 256 failing. Second, a pool whose reads reach several servers whose `<n>` differ, such as standbys replaying a migration at different times: reads that don't overlap alternate between them, and the ticket order adopts each as it is, so in the model a migration that one standby replays 0.6 s after the other gave six adoptions in 0.5 s; only `<n>` that only rises gave one. Both are open questions in PR #3's body, with options. **Both answered by the owner, option (b) on the first and option (a) on the second, below.**
  - **An overlapping read never counts (owner decision, option (b), on the first of those questions):** a read of `<n>` that overlapped the last applied read, its ticket below the horizon, never counts, its `<n>` greater or not: it counts as the last-read `<n>`, applies nothing and sets no horizon, still two words and one atomic add. A read sent after the last applied read arrived is applied and sets the horizon, so the applied reads' snapshots come in order, and in a pool that reaches one server the last-read `<n>` is always the `<n>` the database held at the latest applied read's snapshot, a lowering made while reads are in flight included. A migration seen only by an overlapping read is learned at the next read that doesn't overlap, and meanwhile that call is judged by the older `<n>`. T-0319's span after a lowering and its exception go: I1, I3 and I4 are checked after every event with no span, and I10 now says the last-read `<n>` changes only to the `<n>` of a read whose snapshot is newer than every applied read's. T-0318 refuses an overlapping greater `<n>`, a migration only it saw, and adopts it at the next read, and replays the open question's trace, a read from before a lowering arriving after the lowered `<n>` was adopted, with one action; T-0319 adds a fifth read-order fault switch, an overlapping read counting when its `<n>` is greater, the procedure as it stood at `1212993` (R-1270, R-0981, R-0982, R-1246, R-1271, T-0318, T-0319).
  - **A pool that reaches several servers only rises (owner decision, option (a), on the second):** a pool can reach more than one server when it's `read_only`, or its host list, in its URL or its `host` field, names more than one host and its `target_session_attrs` isn't `"read-write"` or `"primary"`; R-1270 keeps that as one bit set when the pool starts. In such a pool an applied read moves the last-read `<n>` only when its `<n>` is greater, and a lower one counts as the last-read `<n>`, still setting the horizon, so standbys replaying one migration at different times give one adoption, one new epoch and one action through a closed window for it. Those pools alone give up adopting a lowered `<n>` after a restore or a failover until `<n>` passes the old value: meanwhile a migration raising `<n>` back to a value already read counts as no move and is handled as a change made by hand, starting no epoch, so an epoch in force runs to its end, keeping the old `g<n>` behind PgBouncer, removing no entry and letting no action through a closed window, its stale statements repaired through the gate within `stale_cooldown` of the first confirmation or `0A000` and its stale sessions by R-0981's epoch from `stale_cooldown` after it, so R-1271's bounds hold, as for any change made by hand. A process started after the lowering takes it at its pool's start read. R-1288 is the loud README notice beside `read_only` and host lists: what it does, when it's right, what such a pool gives up, and how to proceed, by restarting the processes or giving a pool that writes `target_session_attrs = "read-write"`. T-0318 checks which settings make a pool reach several servers, a lower `<n>` refused there, standbys alternating every 0.1 s with one replaying a migration 0.6 s after the other giving one adoption, one epoch and one action, and migrations raising a lowered `<n>` back counting as no move until one passes it; T-0319's model gets pools that reach two or three servers, standbys replaying each migration and lowering at their own delay up to 0.8 s, I1 for such pools, unless the last-read `<n>` rose between two actions, I10 for them, the last-read `<n>` never going down, and a sixth read-order fault switch, a pool that reaches several servers following the one-server order, and its unit test a row D1 read finding a lower `<n>` in such a pool (R-1270, R-0981, R-0982, R-1246, R-1271, R-1288, T-0318, T-0319).
  - **Checked against a small model** of R-1270's `read_n`, `arrive`, `on_moved`, `request`, `take` and R-0981's epoch test, in both modes, with reads sent, snapshotted and applied apart, each read's snapshot at a random moment of its flight, run with the procedure as it stood at `1212993` and as written now, `stale_cooldown = "1s"` and a pool `max` of 3. The traces, the same in both modes:
    - The first question's trace, a window under `<n>` = 5, a read sent at 0 s seeing 5 and arriving at 0.2 s, a lowering to 3 at 0.05 s, a read sent at 0.06 s seeing 3 and arriving at 0.07 s, and an unread request at 0.21 s, each read making a request: at `1212993` three actions, at 0.07, 0.2 and 0.215 s, the last-read `<n>` going 5, 3, 5, 3; now one, at 0.07 s, the late read refused and the requests due, taken by the first checkout after the window, at 1.07 s.

    Random sequences, 256 of 2,000 events each per mode, a call about every 20 ms, a read's latency 1 ms to 200 ms, 2% of events a migration and 1% a lowering, checking I1, I3 and I4 with no span and I10 as T-0319 now states them, I4 counting the changes of `<n>` within `stale_cooldown`, and one second before it, as migrations: in a pool that reaches one server, at `1212993` no sequence fails with migrations only or with lowerings while no read is in flight, and with lowerings while reads are, all 256 fail I1 and I10 in each mode, I3 in 253 straight to Postgres and I4 in 19; now none fails in any of the three. In pools that reach two or three servers, at `1212993`, and with option (b) alone, every sequence fails I1 and I10 in both modes, with migrations only and with lowerings; now none does. How soon a migration is learned now, in the one-server sequences with lowerings: of 27,689 values of `<n>` some read saw while the database held them, every one held longer than 0.21 s was learned, within 0.281 s of the first read that saw it arriving, after no refused read in 15,465 cases, one in 5,977, and at most 13 at that load; the 1,182 not learned were held no longer than 0.208 s before the next change, or were a sequence's last value with no read sent after the last applied one before it ended. Each fault switch, on the procedure as written now, one-server sequences with lowerings while reads are in flight, straight to Postgres and behind PgBouncer, out of 256: reads applied as they arrive, 256 and 256, by I1 and I10; reads ordered by when they were sent, 184 and 191, by I1 and I10; `<n>` that only rises in a pool that reaches one server, 163 and 155, by I10; the horizon set only by a moving read, 32 and 35, by I10; an overlapping read counting when its `<n>` is greater, 256 and 256, by I1 and I10; and a pool that reaches several servers following the one-server order, 256 and 256 with two servers and migrations only, and with three and lowerings, by I1 and I10.
