Nothing here rests on how Postgres behaves, so nothing ran on it for the two decisions; the model was deleted.
  - **Raised by this round, for the owner:** a pool whose one host reaches several servers at once. The rule above sees only `read_only` and a host list in morm's own settings, but one host can stand for several servers: a PgBouncer database entry listing several hosts, which PgBouncer round-robins by default; a TCP load balancer; or a DNS name with several addresses, which PgBouncer also round-robins and `tokio-postgres` 0.7.18 tries in order, or shuffled with a URL's `load_balance_hosts=random` (`src/connect.rs`, lines 100 to 110). Such a pool, not `read_only`, follows the one-server order, so while its servers' `<n>` differ, as standbys replaying a migration, each read that reaches the other server moves `<n>`, the churn the second decision ends for the pools it names. Evidence, on throwaway servers under `.claude/scratch/` with `TMPDIR` there: two Postgres 18.6 servers, the Manjaro package `18.6-1`, one on 127.0.0.1 and one on 127.0.0.2, their `_morm` comments `morm statement generation 7` and `6`, behind PgBouncer 1.26.1 built from pgbouncer.org's `pgbouncer-1.26.1.tar.gz` (SHA-256 `00886a2994b4844cd37110970df777772cfc42a74c400522c82fba509a3f42d9`, checked against its `.sha256`), in transaction mode with one database entry `host=127.0.0.1,127.0.0.2`: with `load_balance_hosts` at its default, `round-robin`, three clients reading the comment 20 times each got `7` 39 times and `6` 21 times, and one client connection reading it 30 times in a row got `6` each time, so a pool with one host, PgBouncer, can see two values of `<n>`; with `load_balance_hosts=disable` all 90 reads got `7`. Not run: Postgres 17, PgBouncer 1.22, a load balancer, and a DNS name with several addresses, whose behaviour is from PgBouncer's `doc/config.md` and `tokio-postgres`'s source. An open question in PR #3's body, with options. **Answered by the owner, option (b), below.**
  - **A `servers` pool setting (owner decision, option (b), on that question):** each pool gets `servers = "auto" | "one" | "several"`, new R-1289 in step 14, set in `[pool]`, a `[pools.<name>]` profile, which takes `[pool]`'s value when it sets none, or `MORM_POOL_SERVERS`, new R-1290 in step 10, for the startup pool. `"auto"`, the default, keeps the rule above; `"several"` declares a pool whose one host reaches several servers, a PgBouncer entry listing several hosts, a load balancer or a DNS name with several addresses, so `<n>` only rises there; `"one"` declares one server. R-1270's servers bit is set from it when the pool starts. R-0060 gains the `servers = "auto"` line in `[pool]`, copied into steps 11, 12, 27 and 47; R-1273 and T-0320 give it a `# WARNING:` line in the shipped `morm.toml` for `"one"`; R-1262 says `servers` is a pool setting the machine reads but not a tunable; R-1288's loud notice tells pools behind such a host to set `servers = "several"` and says what `"one"` does, when it's right and what it costs; new R-1291 is its "use this when" line. T-0318 checks the bit for each value and a lower `<n>` refused with `"several"` on one host and adopted with `"one"` on a `read_only` pool; new T-0329 checks the loader: the default, inheritance and overrides, invalid values, the key in the wrong table, `server` for `servers`, and each value's bit. This round's own choices, for the owner to approve through PR #3: `"one"` wins over `read_only` and a host list, with no refusal and no warning at start, as option (b)'s wording has it let a `read_only` pool on one standby follow a lowered `<n>`; other values are `Error::Config`, as option (b) said, naming the key, its table or the variable, and the three values; `servers` inherits from `[pool]` like every pool setting, so the notice says to set `"one"` in the pool's own profile; `MORM_POOL_SERVERS` exists because every other `[pool]` key in R-0060 has its variable; `server` written for `servers`, in `[pool]` or a profile, whose `server` is its `[pools.<name>.server]` table, is refused naming `servers`; and T-0319 doesn't change, because the setting only sets the bit, whose two values its model already drives, the fault switch "a pool that reaches several servers following the one-server order" being what a wrong `"one"` costs. The notice's "seven moves in 0.6 seconds" is the model's figure from the round above for two standbys replaying a migration 0.6 s apart, reads alternating every 0.1 s, at `1212993`: seven moves, from 0.06 to 0.66 s, `<n>` going 2, 1, 2, 1, 2, 1, 2. Those reads don't overlap, so `1212993`'s order and today's one-server order treat them alike, and a pool counted as reaching one server, by `"one"` or by `"auto"` behind such a host, moves as often. Nothing new here rests on how Postgres or PgBouncer behaves, beyond the PgBouncer run above, so nothing ran on them.

- **The seventeenth review, EU1 to EU5** (`reviews/step-55-review-17.md`), which found ET1 and the three decision rounds written in, and two should-fix and three minor findings. After several rounds each found a new concurrency case in the read and gate path (ET1, overlapping reads, several-server pools, EU1), the owner chose one consolidated rewrite of that path over another fix round:
  - **EU1 (owner decision, option (a)):** each stretch of a call's machine work with no I/O in it runs as one step under a short per-pool synchronous lock, never held across I/O or an `.await`, taken only on the stale-session paths; a checkout stays one atomic read of the generation word when nothing is due and takes a due action with one compare-and-swap; `old` judges by the pool's last-read `<n>`. R-1270's read and gate path is now one section: the state table, "Steps and the stale lock" with why no other interleaving matters, the procedure with every I/O point marked, and the read order and several-server paragraphs, each rule stated once with no history. R-0981, R-0982, R-1246 and R-1271 follow; T-0318 gets the threaded cases, and T-0319's events are the machine's steps (R-1270, R-0981, R-0982, R-1246, R-1271, T-0318, T-0319). Earlier bullets here that say an epoch's `T` is "the moment the read arrives" now mean the time of the step that applies it.
  - **The rewrite's own choices,** for the owner to approve through PR #3 (101 to 109 there): the window a checkout's take starts is set in one step, `finish_take`, with a starting bit any step finishes first, because one compare-and-swap can't also set the window; steps change the generation word only by compare-and-swap, and judge again when a checkout's came first; the pending lookup stays lock-free and a confirming run's outcome is judged against the entry its run found; a request carries no `<n>`; behind PgBouncer a request never reads `<n>`, as R-0982's and R-1246's prose said against the procedure's condition; `T` is the time of the step that applies the read, and a failed open suspends only the epoch its own test used; one momentary extra per pool, so concurrent replacements keep R-0981's `max + 1`; a failed read's call goes on judged by the last-read `<n>`; and R-1271's I7 condition, `stale_pending_expiry` at least `stale_cooldown`.
  - **EU2:** R-0981's old-connection bullet now says a read that doesn't count starts an epoch only when any read would, never under the older `<n>` it found, as the review gave; T-0319's unit case names the other call's read as a session-class failure's, and adds the gate's own read, row B1's `0A000` with the window closed, after which the late row B3 read starts the epoch under the new `<n>`.
  - **EU3:** `"primary"` is gone from `"auto"`'s rule in R-1270, R-1289, R-1288 and T-0318. `tokio-postgres` 0.7.18, the version `Cargo.lock` holds, takes only `"any"`, `"read-write"` and `"read-only"` (`src/config.rs`, lines 677 to 686), and a throwaway probe built offline in `.claude/scratch/` against the workspace's `Cargo.lock` parsed `target_session_attrs=primary`, `standby` and `prefer-standby` as `invalid connection string` and the other three as `Any`, `ReadWrite` and `ReadOnly`; the probe was deleted.
  - **EU4:** T-0319's I3 is stated as I1 is, one-server and several-server.
  - **EU5:** R-1288's "What morm can't see" says what a move does in each mode, as the review gave.
  - **Checked against a concurrent model** of the procedure as rewritten, in Python, in `.claude/scratch/` with `TMPDIR` there, deleted after. Every function that does I/O is a coroutine, so each call runs as its steps with I/O gaps between them, and a discrete-event scheduler interleaves many calls at once: a call about every 20 ms and bursts of three to eight calls hitting the same signal at one instant, outside transactions, in `.retry(0)` to `.retry(2)` transactions whose attempts may bind other values, and on held connections. A step runs whole, as the stale lock makes it, except that between a step's reading of the generation word and its compare-and-swap another connection's lock-free checkout can land, half the time. Reads of `<n>` take 1 ms to 200 ms, their snapshot anywhere in their flight, 1% fail; runs take 1 ms to 10 ms, opens 5 ms to 25 ms. The world: migrations (2% of events), lowerings (1%) and changes made by hand (1%) of five kinds, a kept type swap among them; standbys replaying each change of `<n>` up to 0.8 s late in pools reaching two or three servers; stored text holding `_morm_retired_` toggled on and off; a client's bad labels; refused opens; straight to Postgres a connection is its session, and behind PgBouncer three server sessions holding statements by text, reached by the client connection's last one or another, replaced by `server_lifetime` and by `RECONNECT` after half the migrations. The invariants are T-0319's, checked after every step: I1 and R-0982's bound, I2, I3 in both forms, I4 with each open counted at the time of what caused it (the epoch's start, or the action), I5, I6, I7, I8 straight to Postgres, I9 for the kept swap, and I10. Settings: `stale_cooldown = "1s"`, `stale_pending_expiry = "1s"`, a pool of four, a budget of five, and the variants below, each 256 sequences of 2,000 events, about 40 s of pool time each.

The same with the window closed after a read found `<n>` moved from 1 to 2, two calls whose reads were sent together: one action; with no lock, two, against I1.

    - EU2's, an epoch under 1, a session-class read sent at 0 s and applied at 0.1 s, a migration at 0.02 s and the gate's own read sent at 0.03 s and applied at 0.04 s: one *clear* at 0.04 s, and the late read starts an epoch under 2 at 0.1 s, as R-0981 now says.
    - ET1's: one action at 0.061 s and B3's *replace* due, taken by the first checkout after the window at 1.103 s; with a request judged by the `<n>` its call carried, a second action 39 ms later, against I1.

A checkout landing between a step's judging of the gate and its compare-and-swap: one action and the step's *replace* due; with a plain store and no starting bit, two at once, against I1.
  - **Random sequences, the procedure as written,** 256 of 2,000 events per setting: one server, one server with `servers = "several"`, two and three servers, pending entries off, caps of 1, `stale_pending_expiry` a fifth of `stale_cooldown`, the statement caches off, a role limit at the pool's size and a budget of eight, each straight to Postgres, and the same behind PgBouncer but the role limit and the budget. No sequence fails I1 to I6, I8 or I10 in any setting. From 4,400 to 13,300 actions and up to 7,500 epochs per setting, R-0982's bound held throughout. I7 and I9 hold apart from the three cases raised below, which the model counts instead of failing on: straight to Postgres, one to three transactions per 256 sequences whose `.retry(1)` ended in `Error::SchemaChanged` because another call's confirming run removed the entry, and up to two because a pool-wide action or a move removed it after the attempt's checkout; behind PgBouncer with one server, 58 transactions under `.retry(1)` and one under `.retry(2)` on a marker that returned to a `g<n>` used before, 872 silent wrong answers from the kept swap on such a text, 10 from a removed entry and three from an action or a move after the checkout; behind PgBouncer with two or three servers, where `<n>` only rises, no returned marker, 5 to 13 from a removed entry and up to three from an action. With `stale_pending_expiry` shorter than `stale_cooldown` an expired entry gave one more straight and 17 behind PgBouncer, which R-1271 now puts outside I7's conditions.
  - **The recommended options to the three questions,** modelled together: a cached run clearing its caches when its connection is behind the statement generation, a fresh salt on adopting an `<n>` no greater than every one adopted, and an entry kept to its expiry when a confirming run's outcome removed it. With all three, no transaction's `.retry(n)` ended in a stale statement with one, two or three servers, straight or behind PgBouncer, apart from two in the pool on one server declared `servers = "several"` behind PgBouncer, an entry expired and one removed by an action while the attempt's run was in flight, and no silent wrong answer came past I9's bound; with caps of 1, outside I7's conditions, 2 straight and 14 behind PgBouncer remained per 256 sequences. With the first two alone, behind PgBouncer with one server, the returned marker's cases and silent answers went to none, the removed entry's stayed, 13 per 256, and one action came while an attempt's run was in flight, which the first option can't reach, as for any call already running.

The first figure of each pair is straight to Postgres and the second behind PgBouncer.

Nothing here rests on how Postgres or PgBouncer behaves, beyond EU3's driver probe, so nothing ran on them.
  - **Raised by this round, for the owner:** three cases where a stale statement can reach a caller under `.retry(1)`, against R-1271's "none visible under `.retry(n)`", none from the lock and each needing only an I/O gap one call already has: an attempt checked out before a pool-wide action or a move of `<n>` and run after it; behind PgBouncer, a marker that returns to a `g<n>` used before, after a lowering, which also brings back silent wrong answers past I9's bound; and a pending entry removed by another call's confirming run. R-1271 and T-0319 state the three as the procedure stands. Open questions in PR #3's body, with options. **Answered since by the owner, option (a) each, below.**
- **The three stale-copy cases, owner decisions, option (a) each,** on the rewrite's three open questions. Written into R-1270's one section, with R-0718, R-0982, R-1246, R-1271, R-1269, R-1253, R-1258, R-1268, T-0318 and T-0319:
  - **A cached run checks the generation word.** The word holds the pending entry count beside the two generations, so the pending check is still one relaxed atomic read, and a connection behind either generation clears both caches before the run, so an attempt checked out before a pool-wide action never runs a copy the action was taken to clear.
  - **A fresh salt on adopting an `<n>` that isn't above every one adopted,** behind PgBouncer: a new state word, the highest adopted `<n>`, set by the start read and raised by `on_moved`, so a pool's marker never returns to a text it has used. R-1258's README notice says to send `RECONNECT` after lowering the statement generation while PgBouncer's server connections stay up, because a process started afterwards uses `g<n>` unsalted.
  - **A confirming run ends only an entry's confirming role:** one that fails the same way or ends on the no-confirm list clears the entry's bytes and leaves the entry to its expiry, or to an action or a moved `<n>`.
  - **This round's own choices,** for the owner to approve through PR #3 (110 to 117 there): the count kept in the generation word and changed by compare-and-swap; a connection behind the connection generation clearing its caches too, its replacement still at the next checkout; the start read counting as adopted, and the salt covering a migration back to a held value; the `RECONNECT` paragraph in R-1258 naming a dump restored into the live database; `end_confirm` for every confirming run, the transaction's own included; R-1271, R-1253 and R-1268 stating what's left as the procedure stands; T-0319's wider gaps for the new fault switches and its two-statement transactions.

So restoring a dump lowers `<n>` with every session up. Not run: Postgres 17 (only 18.6 is installed here) and PgBouncer (not installed, and its sharing of server statements by text is proof 16's).
  - **The concurrent model, rebuilt.** The earlier round's model was deleted with its scratch, so this round wrote it again, in Python, in `.claude/scratch/` with `TMPDIR` there, deleted after, from R-1270's procedure as now written: each step a stretch of code with no yield, so steps never interleave, as the stale lock makes them, and each I/O a yield to a discrete-event scheduler, so other calls' steps, reads and checkouts run between two steps of one call; a lock-free checkout's compare-and-swap lands between a step's read of the generation word and its own half the time. The world: a call about every 20 ms, bursts of three to eight calls with the same statement and values at one instant, 55% outside a transaction, 35% in `.retry(0)` to `.retry(2)` transactions of one statement or, in 30% of them, two, whose attempts rebind other values 30% of the time, and 10% on held connections straight to Postgres; reads of `<n>` 1 ms to 200 ms with the snapshot anywhere in flight, 1% failing; runs 1 ms to 10 ms, 3% ending on the no-confirm list; opens 5 ms to 25 ms; migrations (2% of events, half refreshing functions, `RECONNECT` after half behind PgBouncer), lowerings (1%), changes made by hand of five kinds (1%: a swap dropping the type, one keeping it without a relabel, one with a relabel, a column change, and one changing nothing); stored text holding `_morm_retired_` toggled; refused opens for up to 0.5 s; servers replaying each change of `<n>` up to 0.8 s late; statements: an enum cast touching no table, one whose result type a column change alters, a plpgsql `DECLARE v st` function, a record-shape function and a function converting stored text; client labels good, only in the new type, bad, of another enum, holding `_morm_retired_`, and hitting a unique violation; straight to Postgres a connection is its session, behind PgBouncer three server sessions holding statements by text, 16 each, replaced after 4 s and by `RECONNECT`, the client's cached statement keeping the text it was prepared under. The oracle is a fresh session with a fresh statement. Settings as the earlier round's: `stale_cooldown` and `stale_pending_expiry` `"1s"`, a pool of four, a budget of five; one server, one with `servers = "several"`, two and three servers, pending entries off, caps of 1, `stale_pending_expiry` a fifth of `stale_cooldown`, the caches off, a role limit at the pool's size and a budget of eight, each straight to Postgres, and the same but the last two behind PgBouncer: 18 settings, 256 sequences of 2,000 events each. - **The eighteenth review, EV1 to EV7** (`reviews/step-55-review-18.md`). It found the three decision rounds written in, two should-fix findings and five minor ones. The owner chose option (a) on EV2 and on EV4. Written into R-1270's one section, with R-0718, R-0982, R-1246, R-1271, R-1269, T-0318 and T-0319:
  - **EV1:** a statement generation is raised only after the marker it's raised for is set, in the same step. A checkout's compare-and-swap sets only the starting bit, once a due action's window has ended and the bit is clear. `finish_take` does the take in one step: a *salt*'s fresh salt, then the raise with the due and starting bits cleared, then the window. `take` sets a *salt*'s salt before its own compare-and-swap.
  - **EV2 (owner decision, option (a)):** a request made while its call's transaction is open, a confirming run's inside a transaction, never reads `<n>`, in either mode. It's judged by the pool's last-read `<n>`. The reads after a rollback, rows B2, B3, B4 and D1 and row B1's request, and every read outside a transaction, are unchanged. The read order's premise, that every read runs on a snapshot of its own, is now stated.
  - **EV3:** lock-free reads of the generation word and the window's end are acquire reads, and compare-and-swaps on the word are acquire-release. A prepare's read of the marker, behind PgBouncer, is the fourth thing that runs with no lock, through a sequence lock steps write. A new bullet says why a connection that sees a raised generation sees its marker. T-0318 gets a `loom` case.
  - **EV4 (owner decision, option (a)):** a start read that fails fails the pool's start with its error, as a failed first connection does. So the last-read `<n>` is set before any call reaches the gate.
  - **EV5:** R-1271's four per-kind bullets, and T-0319's I7 headline and I8 stale-session clause, count the attempts "One attempt per stale thing" counts.
  - **EV6:** T-0319's stale-lock switch is split in two: a take never finished by a step, with no starting bit; and a plain store into the word. With EV1's and EV2's switches, T-0319 states 28 in all. The earlier rounds here ran 20, 23 and 26 switches, which match T-0319 as it stood once split.
  - **EV7:** the PR body only.
  - **This round's own choices,** for the owner to approve through PR #3 (126 to 136 there):
    - the marker read as a sequence lock;
    - `take`'s salt before its compare-and-swap;
    - `finish_take`'s order;
    - the read's step finishing a begun take, and an adoption replacing one (below);
    - row B1's request, made after the rollback, still reading;
    - EV2 in both modes;
    - EV4's shape, after step 41's check;
    - `loom` under `--cfg morm_loom`;
    - I2 for the plain-store switch;
    - the model's isolation levels, lock wait and EV2 stress;
    - EV5's wording in I7's headline too.
- **Postgres 17.11 (a local build at `/opt/pgsql-17`, not built from a tarball this round) and 18.6 (the Manjaro package `18.6-1`).** These were throwaway clusters under the worktree's `.claude/scratch/`, on Unix sockets only (`listen_addresses = ''`), with `TMPDIR` there and the sockets reached through a short path under `~/.claude/scratch/`, because the worktree's path passes the socket's 107-byte limit. One session ran `BEGIN ISOLATION LEVEL <level>`, another committed `COMMENT ON SCHEMA _morm IS 'morm statement generation <k+1>'`, and the first read the comment from `pg_description` and through `obj_description`, then ran `ROLLBACK` and read it again. On both versions:
  - **READ COMMITTED** read the new value inside the transaction, whether or not a statement ran first.
  - **REPEATABLE READ and SERIALIZABLE** read the old value when a statement had run before the commit. They read the new value when the read was the transaction's first statement, since the snapshot is the first statement's.
  - **After `ROLLBACK`** every level read the new value.
  - **A read after an error,** in a REPEATABLE READ transaction whose statement had failed with `22012`, got `25P02`, and after `ROLLBACK` it read the new value.

  So a confirming run's request inside an open REPEATABLE READ or SERIALIZABLE transaction would read an old `<n>`, and every read the procedure makes after a rollback sees the committed one.
- **PgBouncer 1.26.1, built from pgbouncer.org's `pgbouncer-1.26.1.tar.gz`** (SHA-256 `00886a2994b4844cd37110970df777772cfc42a74c400522c82fba509a3f42d9`, checked against its `.sha256` and the hash recorded above). One ran in front of each Postgres, in transaction mode, with `max_prepared_statements = 100` and one server connection, on Unix sockets.
  - **The setup:** client A prepared `/* morm g1.1111 */ SELECT (($1::text)::ev1st)::text` and ran it. Then a swap made by hand dropped the enum `ev1st` and created it again.
  - **The old text:** A's prepared copy, and two new client connections each preparing the same text, all got `XX000` `cache lookup failed for type`.
  - **The new text:** each new client preparing `/* morm g1.2222 */ ...` got the right answer.
  - **The same on both versions.** That's EV1's premise: a connection that prepares the old text after the change, under any generation, meets the stale server statement.
  - **What wasn't run:** PgBouncer 1.20.1 and 1.22, and Postgres 15 and 16, which proof 16 and the building steps' tests cover. The servers are stopped and the scratch is deleted.
- **The concurrent model, rebuilt.** The last round's model was deleted with its scratch, so this round wrote it again, in Python, in the worktree's `.claude/scratch/` with `TMPDIR` there, deleted after, from R-1270's procedure as now written.
  - **How it runs.** Each call is a generator, and the code between two yields is one step, so steps never interleave, as the stale lock makes them. Each I/O is a yield to a discrete-event scheduler: a read of `<n>`, a run, an open, a rollback and the lock wait.
  - **The lock-free work** runs inline: a checkout's read and compare-and-swap, the pending check, and a prepare's read of the marker.
  - **Between a checkout's compare-and-swap and its `finish_take`** there's a lock wait of 0 to 0.5 ms, during which other calls' checkouts, cached runs and prepares go on.
  - **Between a step's read of the generation word and its compare-and-swap,** another connection's checkout lands half the time.
  - **The world.**
    - **Calls:** one about every 20 ms, with bursts of three to eight calls with the same statement and values. 55% run outside a transaction and 35% in `.retry(0)` to `.retry(2)` transactions of one statement or, in 30%, two. The other 10% are on held connections straight to Postgres, each holding one to three statements 10 ms to 250 ms apart.
    - **Isolation:** half the attempts run at REPEATABLE READ or SERIALIZABLE, with the snapshot at the attempt's first statement.
    - **Timings:** reads of `<n>` take 1 ms to 200 ms, with the snapshot anywhere in flight, and 1% fail. Runs take 1 ms to 10 ms, a few end on the no-confirm list, and 2% inside transactions end in a serialization failure. Opens take 5 ms to 25 ms.
    - **World events,** at 5.5% of events: migrations (half refreshing functions, `RECONNECT` after half behind PgBouncer), lowerings, changes made by hand of five kinds (a swap dropping the type, one keeping it, one relabelling it, a column change and one changing nothing), stored text holding `_morm_retired_` toggled, and refused opens for up to 0.5 s straight to Postgres. Servers replay each change of `<n>` up to 0.8 s late.
    - **The statements:** an enum cast touching no table, one whose result type a column change alters, a plpgsql `DECLARE v st` function, a record-shape function and a function converting stored text. Client labels are good, only in the new type, bad, holding `_morm_retired_`, or hitting a unique violation.
    - **Connections:** straight to Postgres a connection is its session. Behind PgBouncer three server sessions hold statements by text, 16 each, replaced after 4 s and by `RECONNECT`, and a client's prepare of a text gets the server statement held for it.
  - **The oracle** is a fresh session with a fresh statement, at the run's own instant. A run in flight while a change commits is left out, because no design can repair it.
  - **The invariants,** checked after every step:
    - I1 and I3, consecutive pairs excused by a change of `<n>` after the first one's last-read snapshot;
    - I2, the first checkout after a window takes the due action;
    - I4, I5, I6 and I7;
    - I8: straight to Postgres, no stale session visible from `W` after the last change; no no-retry transaction failing twice on a stale statement inside `stale_pending_expiry` with no action, move or change between, when its entry was made; and the clearing clause, checked against each generation raise;
    - I9;
    - I10, in both forms, with a read that overlapped none setting the last-read `<n>` in a one-server pool.
  - **The settings,** each 256 sequences of 2,000 events: one server, one with `servers = "several"`, two and three servers, pending entries off, caps of 1, `stale_pending_expiry` a fifth of `stale_cooldown`, the caches off, a role limit at the pool's size, and a budget of eight, each straight to Postgres; and the same, but the last two, behind PgBouncer. That's 18 settings, with `stale_cooldown` and `stale_pending_expiry` at `"1s"`, a pool of four and a budget of five.
  - **Left out, as before:** several processes behind one PgBouncer, a `search_path` set per transaction, several pools in one process, a standby's recovery conflict and the transient `XX000`. Memory orderings are left out too: a sequential model can't show them, which is why T-0318 has the `loom` case.
- **Random sequences, the procedure as written.** No sequence fails any invariant in any of the 18 settings. There were 4,500 to 7,800 pool-wide actions per setting straight to Postgres, and 1,650 to 3,050 behind PgBouncer with the caches on, plus 1,300 to 3,100 adoptions, and up to 11,300 epochs. R-0982's bound held throughout.
  - **Isolation:** there were about 207,000 to 267,000 transactions per setting, half their attempts at REPEATABLE READ or SERIALIZABLE. The procedure as written sends no read of `<n>` in an open transaction, and I10 held: no applied read's snapshot was older than one applied before it.
  - **I7:** no stale statement reached a call outside a transaction, no attempt after `SchemaChanged` ran a cached copy, and no transaction got `SchemaChanged` from stale statements twice.
  - **I8's clearing clause:** no cached run used a copy left stale after a generation raised behind PgBouncer, including with checkouts landing in the 0.5 ms before `finish_take`.
- **The traces, each with the design as written and with its fault:**
  - **EV1's,** behind PgBouncer: every connection holds `C` prepared under `g1.<s1>`. A column change made by hand at 0.5 s leaves those server statements stale, and a confirmation at 0.6 s inside the window makes *salt* due. The window ends at 1.1175 s.
    - **As written:** checkout C's compare-and-swap at 1.1180 s sets only the starting bit. `finish_take` at 1.1188 s sets `g1.<s2>` and then raises the generation. C clears its caches and gets the right answer under the new text. Checkout D's run at 1.1183 s, before `finish_take`, still runs the old copy, as any run before the action does. At 1.5 s D gets the right answer.
    - **With the compare-and-swap raising the generation itself:** C clears its caches at 1.1180 s and prepares `g1.<s1>` again under the new generation, against I8's clearing clause.
  - **EV2's,** straight to Postgres: an action at 0 s under 1, so the window is closed to 1 s. A `.retry(1)` REPEATABLE READ transaction T's second attempt starts at 0.2 s, and a migration commits at 0.25 s. Another call's request reads and applies 2 at 0.2717 s and takes *clear*. A swap made by hand keeps the old type at 0.29 s, and X's `22P02` at 0.30 s makes the entry with T's values.
    - **As written:** T's confirming run at 0.353 s confirms, makes no read, and its *clear* is due under 2. A request at 0.3712 s reads 2 and is due too: two actions in all.
    - **With the request reading in T:** it reads 1 from T's snapshot at 0.356 s and applies it, `<n>` going from 2 to 1, against I10.
  - **EV4's:** the start read fails at 0.0177 s.
    - **As written:** the pool's start fails and no call runs.
    - **With the pool starting with the last-read `<n>` unset:** a session-class failure's failed read at 0.11 s starts an epoch under no `<n>`, and the next read, applied at 0.21 s with 1, starts another, against I3.
  - **The adoption beside a begun take,** behind PgBouncer: a *salt* is due, its window ends at 1.0 s, a migration commits at 0.95 s, a checkout's compare-and-swap lands at 1.0005 s, and a read arrives at 1.001 s with 2.
    - **As written:** the read's step finishes the begun take first under 1, then adopts 2, and the marker is `g2` with no salt.
    - **With `finish_take` run inside `on_moved`:** the salt is taken under 2 beside the adoption, the marker is `g2.<salt>`, and I1 fails.
  - **The earlier rounds' traces, rebuilt, each as before:**
    - EU1's eight requests for *replace* at once with the window open: one action at 0.010 s (no lock: a second at 0.0116 s, against I1).
    - Two reads sent together after a move with the window closed: one action at 0.03 s and the other due. The no-lock form isn't reproduced by this trace; the random runs catch it.
    - Eight checkouts at once after a window with a *clear* due: one action at 1.0502 s (a checkout's plain read and write: two, against I1).
    - A checkout landing between a step's judging and its compare-and-swap: one action, the step's *replace*, with the due *clear* folded in. With no starting bit there are two at once, against I1. A plain store gives one action here, and the random runs catch it by I2.
    - EU1's epochs: with A's result held 4 ms, one epoch, at 0.013 s under 2 (`old` in its own step: a second 1 ms later, against I3).
    - EU2's: one *clear* at 0.04 s, and the late read's epoch under 2 at 0.1 s.
    - ET1's: one action at 0.061 s under 2 and the late request's *replace* due, taken by the checkout at 1.1038 s (with the carried `<n>`: a second action 39 ms after the first, against I1).
    - The ticket order's, straight and behind PgBouncer: an action or adoption at 0.07 s (reads applied as they arrive: the last-read `<n>` back to 1 at 0.1 s, against I10).
    - The lowering's: one action at 0.07 s and the last-read `<n>` 3 (an overlapping greater read counting: back to 5, against I10).
    - A migration seen only by an overlapping read: adopted at 0.31 s (counting it: at 0.2 s).
    - Two standbys 0.6 s apart, straight and behind PgBouncer: one action or adoption at 0.06 s (the one-server order: back to 1 at 0.16 s, against I10).
    - The restore to 3, then 4, 5 and 6, in a several-server pool: no move until 6, with the gate's actions at 0.012, 1.012, 2.012, 3.012 and 4.013 s (the one-server order: 5 lowered to 3 at 0.011 s, against I10).
    - A no-retry transaction on a connection checked out before another call's confirmation took the action, straight and behind PgBouncer: its connection clears its caches and it's right (with the generation check off: the stale copy, against I8).
    - A lowered `<n>` adopted behind PgBouncer after a kept swap: the marker `g1.<salt>` and the right answer (with no fresh salt: `g1` again and the server statement another process re-planned under it, against I8).
    - A confirming run that times out: the entry stays with no bytes, and the next no-retry transaction, with other values, is right (removing it: that transaction fails again 0.402 s after the first, against I8).
    - A move of `<n>` between two attempts whose two statements are stale: one `SchemaChanged`, then both right, unnamed (running cached: against I7).
    - A change and a serialization failure between `.retry(2)`'s attempts: one `SchemaChanged`, the serialization failure, then the right answers (only the next attempt unnamed: against I7).
    - A stale statement and a `%ROWTYPE` function compiled before the same column change: `.retry(1)` gets `SchemaChanged` twice and returns it, and `.retry(2)` answers on its third attempt on a newly opened connection.
    - Four replacements under a role limit at the pool's size: 5 opens (with the extra tried after `53300`: 8).
- **Each fault switch is caught,** out of 256 sequences, by the invariant T-0319 names, the first figure straight to Postgres and the second behind PgBouncer:
  - **The design's machines:** no gate, 256 and 256, I1; no epoch, 84, I4; `42804` and `XX000` ungated, 256, I1; an epoch that never ends, 67, I8; no replacement, 210, I8; a deferred request dropped, 256 and 256, I2; no fresh run inside the window, 252 and 222, I7; a request judged by the `<n>` its call carried, 104 and 45, I1.
  - **The read orders:** reads applied as they arrive, 247 and 50, I10; `<n>` only rising in a one-server pool, 256 and 240, I10; reads ordered by when they were sent, 103 and 14, I10; the horizon set only by a moving read, 16 and 3, I10; an overlapping read counting when greater, 141 and 13, I10; a several-server pool following the one-server order, 256 and 248 with two servers, I10; and EV2's new one, a request in an open transaction reading `<n>` in its snapshot, 2, I10, with its stress (90% of calls in two-statement transactions, statements up to 0.3 s apart, a call every 40 ms, migrations and kept swaps at 20% of events).
  - **The stale lock:** no stale lock, 32 straight, I1, 0 behind PgBouncer; `old` in its own step, 244, I3; a checkout's plain read and write, 16 and 18, I1; no starting bit, 23 and 5, I1; a plain store into the word, 8 and 7, I2, and none by I1; EV1's new one, a checkout's compare-and-swap raising the generation itself, 25 behind PgBouncer, I8; two momentary extras, 180 with a budget of eight, I5.
  - **The stale-copy rules:** a cached run skipping the generation check, 218 and 173 with up to 50 ms between a checkout and an attempt's first statement, I8; no fresh salt on a non-rising adoption, 51 behind PgBouncer, I8; a confirming run's outcome removing the entry, 2 behind PgBouncer, with no-retry transactions, 50 ms gaps and 15% of runs on the no-confirm list, I8.
  - **The retry and slot rules:** an attempt after `SchemaChanged` running cached statements, 256 and 256, I7; only the attempt right after `SchemaChanged` unnamed, 256 and 256, I7, with `.retry(2)` and 25% serialization failures; a `53300` leaving the epoch's later replacements trying the extra, 3, I4, under a role limit at the pool's size.
  - **The earlier text this round replaced,** `finish_take` run inside `on_moved`: 6 of 256 behind PgBouncer with one server, I1, and 0 with two servers. Its trace shows it every time.
  - **With each stressed switch's settings and no fault,** no sequence fails.
- **Raised by this round, for the owner:** nothing. The model's one finding, the adoption beside a begun take, is closed in R-1270 with no design choice, because the adoption already took the due action's place. No bound needs a design choice.
- **The nineteenth review, EW1 to EW7** (`reviews/step-55-review-19.md`). It found the eighteenth round written in, three should-fix findings and four minor ones. The owner chose option (a) on EW1 and on EW2. Written into R-1270's one section, with R-0718, R-0981, R-0982, R-1245, R-1246 and T-0318 (step 14), R-0436, R-0441, R-0984, R-1271, T-0312 and T-0319 (step 20), two new loud README notices, R-1292 and R-1293, and a bullet in R-1253 (step 50), and `contracts.md`'s comments on `tx.transaction(..)` and `SchemaChanged`:
  - **EW1 (owner decision, option (a)):** inside a transaction, every row that returns `Error::SchemaChanged` or reads `<n>`, B1 to B4, D1, D2 outside the cool-down and the transient `XX000`, first rolls the whole transaction back, savepoints included, before anything else. The procedure marks it as an I/O line. Nothing more is sent on that transaction, and it returns `Error::SchemaChanged` even when the outer closure catches it and returns `Ok`. R-0441's exception list gains `SchemaChanged`.
  - **EW2 (owner decision, option (a)):** a request on a `db.hold()` connection never reads `<n>`; it's judged by the pool's last-read `<n>`. `25P02` joins R-1245's no-confirm list, and row A1's fresh run, and the transient `XX000`'s retry, that get it return the original error.
  - **EW3:** each fault test first runs its fault's own committed shrunk sequence, then 256 random sequences at a named mode and setting. T-0319 names each switch's, each one under which the model below caught it in at least 30 of 256.
  - **EW4:** every take a step finishes is taken under the last-read `<n>` that step started with. This is wider than the review's proposal, which was to make `on_moved`'s entry removal before it sets `<n>`; the trace below shows why.
  - **EW5:** the sequence lock's writer has a release fence after the odd store, and the data words are relaxed atomics. The `loom` case drives the module's own atomics through a shim, asserts a whole marker whatever generation a prepare read, and has a fault form without the fence. `morm` declares `cfg(morm_loom)`.
  - **EW6:** pending entries live in a fixed array of `stale_pending_max` four-word slots, each a sequence lock. The lookup is the fifth lock-free thing.
  - **EW7:** the churn bounds hold per epoch, per *replace* action, and over any span of `stale_cooldown` (`4 × max + 4` opens, plus `2 × max` per migration). T-0319's I4 checks all three.
  - **This round's own choices,** for the owner to approve through PR #3 (137 to 146 there).
  - **Raised for the owner:** one question, below.
- **Postgres 17.11 (the local build at `/opt/pgsql-17`, not built from a tarball this round) and 18.6 (the Manjaro package `18.6-1`).** These were throwaway clusters under the worktree's `.claude/scratch/`, Unix sockets only (`listen_addresses = ''`), with `TMPDIR` there, the sockets under a short path in `~/.claude/scratch/`, and `\set VERBOSITY verbose` for the SQLSTATEs. Both versions gave the same results:
  - **EW1, REPEATABLE READ and SERIALIZABLE.** Session A read the `_morm` comment (`1`), and session B then committed `2`. A ran `SAVEPOINT s`, `SELECT 1/0` and `ROLLBACK TO SAVEPOINT s`, and read `1`. A then opened a second savepoint, failed again and ran `ROLLBACK`, and read `2`. After that `ROLLBACK`, A's `ROLLBACK TO SAVEPOINT s2` got `25P01` (`ROLLBACK TO SAVEPOINT can only be used in transaction blocks`), and A's `INSERT` ran on its own and committed, seen from another session. So the whole rollback is what gives a fresh snapshot, and anything morm sent after it would run outside the transaction, which is why R-0441 says nothing more is sent.
  - **EW2.** A session prepared `SELECT c FROM tt` and ran it. It then opened a REPEATABLE READ transaction and ran a first statement. Another session committed `<n>` = 2 and changed `tt.c` to `bigint`. The first session read `1` in its open transaction. Its `EXECUTE` got `0A000` (`cached plan must not change result type`). After that, a `PREPARE` of the same text, a plain run of it and a read of the comment each got `25P02`. After `ROLLBACK` it read `2`. So a fresh run on a held connection inside an app's failed transaction never runs, and gets `25P02`.
  - **tokio-postgres 0.7.18,** the registry source: every `Message::ReadyForQuery(_)` match drops the status byte, and no public call returns it.
  - **Not run:** PgBouncer, because nothing this round rests on how it behaves; and Postgres 15 and 16, which T-0312's integration cases cover. The servers are stopped and the scratch deleted.
- **The concurrent model, rebuilt.** The last round's model went with its scratch, so this round wrote it again, in Python, in the worktree's `.claude/scratch/` with `TMPDIR` there, deleted after, from R-1270's procedure as now written. It covers everything the eighteenth and nineteenth reviews' models covered.
  - **How it runs.** Each call is a generator, and the code between two yields is one step, so steps never interleave, as the stale lock makes them. Each I/O is a yield to a discrete-event scheduler: a read of `<n>`, a run, an open, the whole transaction's rollback and the lock wait.
  - **Lock-free work** runs inline: a checkout's read and compare-and-swap, the pending check and lookup, and a prepare's read of the marker. A lock wait of 0 to 0.5 ms comes between a checkout's compare-and-swap and its `finish_take`. Half the time, a step's compare-and-swap that finds a due action's window ended has another connection's checkout land first, and every take a step finishes uses the step's starting last-read `<n>`.
  - **Calls:** one about every 20 ms, in bursts of up to eight with the same statement and values. 55% run outside a transaction, and 35% in `.retry(0)` to `.retry(2)` transactions of one statement or, in 30%, two. About 40% of the attempts are at REPEATABLE READ or SERIALIZABLE, with the snapshot at the attempt's first statement. 30% of statements run inside `tx.transaction(..)`'s savepoint. 10% of calls are on held connections straight to Postgres, half of them inside a REPEATABLE READ transaction the app opened, whose snapshot is its first statement's and whose statements after an error get `25P02`.
  - **Timings:** reads take 1 ms to 200 ms, with the snapshot anywhere in flight, and 1% fail. Runs take 1 ms to 10 ms; 1% end in a statement timeout, and 2% of runs inside transactions end in a serialization failure. Opens take 5 ms to 25 ms.
  - **World events,** at 5.5% of events: migrations (half refreshing functions; behind PgBouncer half followed by `RECONNECT`), lowerings, changes made by hand (a swap dropping the type, one keeping it, a column change, one changing nothing), stored text holding `_morm_retired_` toggled, and refused opens for up to 0.5 s straight to Postgres. Standbys replay each change up to 0.8 s late, in order.
  - **Statements:** an enum cast touching no table, one whose result type a column change alters, a plpgsql `DECLARE v st` function compiled per session, and a record-shape function. Client labels are good, only in the new type, bad, holding `_morm_retired_`, or hitting a unique violation.
  - **Connections:** straight to Postgres a connection is its session, and a replacement beside the old one runs the call before it takes the slot. Behind PgBouncer three server sessions hold statements by text, 16 each, replaced after 4 s and by `RECONNECT`, and a client's prepare of a text gets the server statement held for it.
  - **The oracle** is a fresh session with a fresh statement at the run's own instant. A run in flight while a change commits is left out.
  - **The invariants,** checked at every step:
    - I1 and I3, consecutive pairs excused one for one by a change of `<n>` after the first one's last-read snapshot, or by a rise in a several-server pool;
    - I2, the first checkout after a window with a request due takes an action;
    - I4, per epoch (`max` replacements, `max + 2` opens), per *replace* action (`max` replaced) and over every span of `stale_cooldown` (`4 × max + 4`, plus `2 × max` per migration in the two windows before);
    - I5 and I6;
    - I7's three clauses, with held connections in the app's own transaction left out, as R-1271 states;
    - I8: straight to Postgres no stale session visible outside a transaction from `W` after the last change and refusal, nor under `.retry(2)` with no serialization failure; behind PgBouncer none after `server_lifetime`; no no-retry transaction failing twice on a stale statement whose entry wasn't pushed out, with no action, move or change between; and the clearing clause, against each generation raise;
    - I10, both forms, with a lowered `<n>` from a read that overlapped none adopted in a one-server pool.
  - **I9** is checked only for its source: the only silent wrong answers the model makes are from a kept type's shared label. Its time bounds aren't checked.
  - **The settings,** each 256 sequences of 2,000 events, with `stale_cooldown` and `stale_pending_expiry` at `"1s"`, a pool of four and a budget of five. Each runs straight to Postgres and, where marked, behind PgBouncer:
    - the defaults, `servers = "several"` with one server, and two servers, each in both modes;
    - pending entries off, caps of 1, and `stale_pending_expiry` a fifth of `stale_cooldown`, each in both modes;
    - a role limit at the pool's size, and a budget of eight, straight to Postgres;
    - heavy stress, in both modes: 90% of calls in two-statement transactions, statements up to 0.3 s apart, a call every 40 ms, world events at 20%;
    - savepoints, in both modes: 60% of calls in transactions, 90% of statements inside a savepoint;
    - held connections in the app's transactions, straight to Postgres: 30% of calls held, 80% of those inside the app's REPEATABLE READ transaction;
    - each fault switch's own setting, below, with no fault.
  - **Left out,** as before: several processes behind one PgBouncer, a `search_path` set per transaction, several pools in one process, a standby's recovery conflict, and the transient `XX000`. Memory orderings are left out too, since a sequential model can't show them; that's why T-0318 has the `loom` case. A pool whose start read fails isn't modelled either: every modelled pool starts with its start read applied.
- **Random sequences, the procedure as written.** No sequence fails any invariant in any setting, 38 in all counting the switches' own settings (one I8 failure in the savepoints setting behind PgBouncer was the model's own check, which applied straight to Postgres's healing time to a stale session behind PgBouncer; with `server_lifetime`'s, as R-1271 states it, that setting passes too).
  - **The volume.** Straight to Postgres there were 4,300 to 5,200 pool-wide actions and 4,400 to 5,400 epochs per setting, and 14,700 and 16,200 under stress. Behind PgBouncer there were 2,800 to 3,100 actions and 940 to 1,530 adoptions, and 8,000 and 4,700 under stress. Each setting ran about 500,000 calls and 175,000 transactions (423,000 under stress, 300,000 with savepoints), about 40% of their attempts at REPEATABLE READ or SERIALIZABLE. 65,000 statements per setting ran inside a savepoint (398,000 in the savepoints setting), and 25,000 held calls per setting straight to Postgres ran inside the app's own transaction (121,000 in the held setting). 35 to 490 checkouts landed inside a step's compare-and-swap per setting, 5 to 101 of them inside a step that moved `<n>`.
  - **EW1's and EW2's rules hold:** as written, no read of `<n>` runs in an open transaction, after a rollback only to a savepoint, or in a held connection's open app transaction, and I10 held in every setting.
  - **I7:** no stale statement reached a call outside a transaction, held connections in the app's own transaction apart, no attempt after `SchemaChanged` ran a cached copy, and no transaction got `SchemaChanged` from stale statements twice.
  - **I4** held per epoch, per *replace* action and over every span of `stale_cooldown`, the role limit at the pool's size included.
  - **I8's clearing clause** held in both modes, with checkouts landing in the lock wait before `finish_take`.
- **The traces, each with the design as written and with its fault:**
  - **EW1's,** straight to Postgres. A `.retry(1)` REPEATABLE READ transaction T runs its first statement at 0.10 s under 1. A migration commits at 0.20 s, and a drop made by hand at 0.22 s leaves T's connection's enum cast stale. At 0.27 s another call's session-class failure on stored text reads and applies 2, taking no action. T's second statement, inside a savepoint at about 0.40 s, gets `XX000`.
    - **As written:** the whole rollback, then B2's read, fresh, finds 2 unmoved. T's next attempt runs unnamed and answers. No invariant fails.
    - **With the read made after a rollback only to the savepoint:** it reads 1 in T's snapshot and applies it, `<n>` going from 2 to 1, against I10.
  - **EW2's,** straight to Postgres. A held connection opens the app's REPEATABLE READ transaction at 0.05 s under 1. A migration commits at 0.20 s and is adopted at 0.28 s. A clear is taken under 2 at 0.315 s, closing the window. A kept swap at 0.325 s and a no-retry transaction's `22P02` at 0.34 s make the entry. The held call's confirming run at 0.51 s confirms.
    - **As written:** it makes no read, and its clear is due under 2.
    - **With the request reading:** it reads 1 in the app's snapshot and applies it, against I10.
    - **A held connection's `0A000` inside the app's transaction,** after a column change by hand: the fresh run gets `25P02`, the call returns the original `0A000`, and the `0A000`'s own *clear* is taken with no read.
  - **EW4's,** straight to Postgres. A *clear* is due, its window ended at 1.0 s, and a migration commits at 0.95 s. At 1.055 s a session-class failure's read finds 2 and requests *replace*, and a checkout's compare-and-swap lands at the step's first compare-and-swap after `arrive`, first with a pending entry (at `on_moved`'s removal) and then with none (at the request's `take`).
    - **As written:** in both, the landed *clear* is taken under 1 and the *replace* at once under 2: two actions, the migration excusing the pair.
    - **With the earlier text,** `finish_take` under the current `<n>`: in both, the *clear* is taken under 2 and the *replace* is due until 2.055 s, held back.
    - **With the review's proposed order,** the removal before setting `<n>`: the case with an entry is as written, but the case with none, landing at the `take`, is held back as with the earlier text. So the rule is written for every take a step finishes.
  - **The earlier rounds' traces, rebuilt, each as before:**
    - EV1's, behind PgBouncer: the salt set at 1.1196 s, then the raise; with the compare-and-swap raising, D clears its caches at 1.1180 s and prepares the old text, against I8.
    - EV2's: T's confirming run makes no read, and its clear is due under 2; with the read in T's snapshot, 1 is applied, against I10.
    - The adoption beside a begun take: the begun take finished under 1, then the adoption of 2, the marker `g2` unsalted.
    - ET1's: one action at 0.061 s and the late request due; with the carried `<n>`, a second action at 0.100 s, against I1.
    - EU1's eight requests at once: one action at 0.010 s; with no lock, two, against I1.
    - Eight checkouts after a window with a *clear* due: one action at 1.0503 s; with a plain read and write, two, against I1.
    - A checkout landing between a step's judging and its compare-and-swap: one action, the due *clear*, and the step's *replace* due; with no starting bit, two at once, against I1.
    - EU1's epochs, A's read held: one epoch at 0.011 s under 2; with `old` in its own step, a second at 0.0125 s, against I3.
    - Two reads sent together after a move with the window closed: one action at 0.03 s and the other due; with no lock, two, against I1.
    - The ticket order's, in both modes: the move at 0.07 s, kept; with no ticket, the late read sets `<n>` back to 1, against I10.
    - The lowering's: one action at 0.07 s and the last-read `<n>` 3; with an overlapping greater read counting, back to 5, against I10.

- **Each fault switch is caught,** out of 256, by the invariant T-0319 names, in the mode and at the setting T-0319 names for it (the defaults where it names none), with the shrunk sequence's length in events. 
  - **The design's machines:** no gate, 256 (7 events) and 256 (9 events) straight and behind PgBouncer, I1; no epoch, 256 (6 events) straight, I4; `42804` and `XX000` ungated, 256 (7 events) straight, I1; an epoch that never ends, 256 (39 events) straight, I8; no replacement, 234 (5 events) straight, I8; a deferred request dropped, 256 (4 events) and 256 (5 events) straight and behind PgBouncer, I2; no fresh run inside the window, 254 (26 events) and 243 (19 events) straight and behind PgBouncer, I7; a request judged by the `<n>` its call carried, 241 (26 events) and 255 (23 events) straight and behind PgBouncer, I1.

  - **The stale lock:** no stale lock, 34 (109 events) straight, I1; `old` in its own step, 218 (27 events) straight, I3; a checkout's plain read and write, 71 (16 events) and 96 (12 events) straight and behind PgBouncer, I1; no starting bit, 125 (83 events) straight, I1; a plain store into the word, 104 (13 events) behind PgBouncer, I2; EV1's, a checkout's compare-and-swap raising the generation, 30 (13 events) behind PgBouncer, I8; two momentary extras, 229 (5 events) straight, I5.

With each switch's own setting and no fault, no sequence fails (above). Each shrunk sequence, run alone with its fault, fails the named invariant. The step's PR commits the Rust simulation's own sequences; this model's lengths only indicate their size.
  - **The earlier text this round replaced,** `finish_take` under the current `<n>` (EW4), breaks no invariant, as the review said. Its trace above shows the held-back action every time, and the random runs had 5 to 101 checkouts per setting land inside a step that moved `<n>`.
- **Raised by this round, for the owner:** whether a stale session returned as it is inside a savepoint, row B4, should fail the whole transaction, as EW1's rollback before R-0981's read now makes it. That's question 1 in the PR body. The plan states it as written, in R-0441 and R-1292's notice, while it's open. **Answered since by the owner, option (b), and written in (below).**
- **A stale-session error inside a savepoint stays catchable, owner decision, option (b),** on the nineteenth fix round's open question (row B4). Written into R-1270's one section, R-0981 and T-0318 (step 14), R-0441, R-0984, R-1271, T-0312 and T-0319 (step 20), and R-1292's loud notice (step 50):
  - **The rule:** inside a nested `tx.transaction(..)` (a savepoint), rows B3 and B4 make no read of `<n>`: R-0981's epoch test judges by the pool's last-read `<n>`, as after a read that applies nothing. So B4 rolls back nothing but the savepoint, and its error, returned as it is, is a Postgres error the savepoint clears, which the app can catch and carry on; B3 still returns `Error::SchemaChanged` and rolls the whole transaction back, after the step that decides it, the connection closed after that rollback. Each call's context carries the savepoint bit. A migration first seen there is learned at the next read of `<n>` outside a transaction, a read after a whole transaction's rollback included, and the epoch in force decides until then. Rows that return `Error::SchemaChanged` still roll the whole transaction back before any read (EW1), and so does B4 outside a savepoint, at the transaction's top level, where the error fails the transaction anyway.
  - **What the app gives up, as R-1271 and R-1292 now state:** after a migration first seen inside a savepoint, a connection opened between the epoch in force's start and the migration isn't old there, so a function the migration left stale returns its error as it is in each savepoint call on that connection, until that epoch ends, within `stale_cooldown` of the migration, or a read outside a transaction starts a new one. From `t0 + W` on no stale session is visible inside a savepoint either, because any epoch in force by then started after the change.
  - **T-0319** gains I11 (a savepoint clears every error the machine returns inside it as it is, and every whole rollback made for a call inside a savepoint returns `Error::SchemaChanged`), "inside a savepoint" in I8's stale-session clause, and a 31st fault switch, the savepoint bit dropped from the call's context, caught by I11.
  - **This round's own choices,** for the owner to approve through PR #3 (147 to 155 there).
  - **Raised for the owner:** nothing.
- **Postgres 17.11 (the local build at `/opt/pgsql-17`) and 18.6 (the Manjaro package `18.6-1`),** throwaway clusters under the worktree's `.claude/scratch/`, Unix sockets only, with `TMPDIR` there; both gave the same results. Session A called a plpgsql `DECLARE v st` function, compiling it; another session dropped and recreated `st` and raised the `_morm` comment to generation 2. A then ran a REPEATABLE READ transaction whose first statement read generation 1: inside `SAVEPOINT s1`, the function failed with `XX000` `cache lookup failed for type`, `ROLLBACK TO SAVEPOINT s1` cleared it, an `INSERT` ran, and a second savepoint failed and was cleared the same way; the comment read after `ROLLBACK TO SAVEPOINT` still gave generation 1; the `COMMIT` kept all three rows. The same call in A after the commit failed again, and a new session's call answered. So a stale session's error inside a savepoint repeats on its connection until the connection goes, a savepoint clears it, the outer transaction commits, and a read there would see the transaction's snapshot: what B4 with no read relies on. PgBouncer wasn't run: nothing this round rests on it. The servers are stopped and the scratch deleted.
- **The concurrent model, rebuilt again** (the last round's went with its scratch), in Python, in the worktree's `.claude/scratch/` with `TMPDIR` there, deleted after, from R-1270's procedure as now written, savepoint bit included.
  - **How it runs,** as before: each call is a generator and the code between two yields is one step, so steps never interleave; each I/O is a yield to a discrete-event scheduler (a read of `<n>`, a run, an open, a rollback, a lock wait), and lock-free work runs inline. A step's compare-and-swap that finds a due action's window ended has another connection's checkout land first half the time (every time where a trace says so), and every take a step finishes uses that step's starting last-read `<n>`.
  - **Calls:** one about every 20 ms, a fifth in bursts of two to eight with the same statement and values; 55% outside a transaction, 35% in `.retry(0)` to `.retry(2)` transactions of one statement or, in 30%, two up to 10 ms apart; 40% of attempts at REPEATABLE READ or SERIALIZABLE, the snapshot at the attempt's first statement; 30% of statements inside `tx.transaction(..)`'s savepoint, whose errors returned as they are the outer closure catches half the time and carries on, committing; 10% of calls on held connections straight to Postgres, half inside a REPEATABLE READ transaction the app opened, whose later statements and reads get `25P02` after an error.
  - **Timings:** reads 1 ms to 200 ms with the snapshot anywhere in flight, 1% failing; runs 1 ms to 10 ms, 1% ending in a statement timeout and 2% of runs inside transactions in a serialization failure; opens 5 ms to 25 ms; a lock wait of up to 0.5 ms between a checkout's compare-and-swap and its `finish_take`.
  - **World events,** at 5.5% of events: migrations (each ends with the reset, so a cached plan is planned again at its next run; half refresh functions, half also change the column's type, and behind PgBouncer half are followed by `RECONNECT`), lowerings, swaps made by hand that drop the type or keep it unrelabelled, column changes by hand, changes that change nothing, stored text holding `_morm_retired_` toggled, and refused opens for up to 0.5 s straight to Postgres. Standbys replay each change, in order, up to 0.8 s late.
  - **Statements:** an enum cast touching no table and an `INSERT` casting to the enum (labels good, only in the new type, bad, holding `_morm_retired_`, or a unique violation), a statement whose result type a column change alters, the same under a per-transaction `search_path`, a plpgsql `DECLARE v st` function and a `%ROWTYPE` one, each compiled per session at its first call, a function converting stored text, and a record-shape function.
  - **Connections:** straight to Postgres a connection is its session; behind PgBouncer three server sessions hold statements by text, 16 each, replaced after 4 s and by `RECONNECT`. The oracle is a fresh session with a fresh statement at the run's own instant; a call in flight while a change commits is left out.
  - **The invariants** are T-0319's, I1 to I11, as the nineteenth round's model checked them, with I8 checked inside savepoints too and I11 new. I9 is checked only for its source, as before.
  - **The settings,** each 256 sequences of 2,000 events, `stale_cooldown` and `stale_pending_expiry` at `"1s"`, a pool of four and a budget of five: the defaults, `servers = "several"` with one server, two servers, pending entries off, caps of 1, `stale_pending_expiry` a fifth of `stale_cooldown`, heavy stress (90% of calls in two-statement transactions up to 0.3 s apart, a call every 100 ms, world events at 20%) and savepoints (60% of calls in transactions, 90% of statements inside a savepoint), each in both modes; a role limit at the pool's size, a budget of eight and held connections in the app's transactions (30% of calls held, 80% of those inside the app's transaction), straight to Postgres; and each fault switch's own setting, below, with no fault: 42 in all.
  - **Left out,** as before: several processes behind one PgBouncer, several pools in one process, a standby's recovery conflict, the transient `XX000`, a pool whose start read fails, and memory orderings, which T-0318's `loom` case covers.
- **Random sequences, the procedure as written.** No sequence fails any invariant in any of the 42 settings. Each base setting ran about 870,000 calls and 305,000 transactions (663,000 under stress, 523,000 with savepoints), about 40% of their attempts at REPEATABLE READ or SERIALIZABLE, and 115,000 statements inside a savepoint (610,000 in the savepoints setting). Straight to Postgres there were 6,700 to 11,600 pool-wide actions and 9,100 to 10,600 epochs per base setting (36,100 and 33,500 under stress); behind PgBouncer 5,000 to 6,600 actions and 2,700 to 4,700 adoptions. Straight to Postgres, 8,300 to 9,200 session-class errors per base setting came back as they are inside a savepoint (51,400 in the savepoints setting), 170 to 580 of them from a stale session (4,200 in the savepoints setting), every one before `t0 + W`, and the outer closure caught 6,800 to 7,200 of them (40,700) and committed. 20 to 2,000 checkouts per setting landed inside a step's compare-and-swap, up to 110 inside a step that moved `<n>`.
  - **The savepoint rule holds:** no session-class failure inside a savepoint read `<n>`, none returned as it is after a whole rollback, every whole rollback for a call inside a savepoint returned `Error::SchemaChanged` (I11), and no stale session was visible inside a savepoint from `t0 + W` (I8).
  - **A finding in the model, not the plan:** one sequence in EW1's switch setting first failed I8 with a stale session's `22P02` returned as it is inside a savepoint 1.1 s after a migration. The model had returned a confirming run's error as it is whenever it confirmed; the procedure sends that error on to `on_error`, as an unnamed run's, so it reaches row B3 on the old connection. With the model fixed to the procedure, every setting passes, that sequence included.
- **The traces, each with the design as written and with its fault:**
  - **B4, a client's stored text inside a savepoint,** straight to Postgres. An epoch starts under 1 at 0.107 s; at 0.32 s a REPEATABLE READ `.retry(1)` transaction on a connection opened since then meets the stored text inside its savepoint.
    - **As written:** `22P02` as it is with no read, the outer closure catches it, runs its next statement and commits.
    - **With the savepoint bit dropped:** the whole rollback and a read at 0.3358 s, then the `22P02` as it is: against I11.
  - **B4, a migration first seen inside a savepoint,** straight to Postgres. The epoch under 1 starts at 0.107 s, and its replacement connection compiles the `DECLARE v st` function at 0.20 s; a migration that refreshes no function commits at 0.30 s.
    - **As written:** at 0.42 s the function's `22P02` inside the savepoint returns as it is with no read, and the transaction commits; a session-class failure outside a transaction at 0.60 s reads 2 and starts an epoch under 2 at 0.61 s, so at 0.82 s the same failure inside a savepoint is row B3: `Error::SchemaChanged`, the whole rollback, the connection discarded, and the next attempt answers on a new one. With no read outside a transaction, it's B4 again at 0.82 s, and B3 at 1.22 s, once the epoch under 1 has ended, in a new epoch under 1.
    - **With the savepoint bit dropped:** B3 at 0.43 s, after the whole rollback's read: the earlier design's outcome, which breaks no invariant here; the stored-text trace above is the one it breaks.
  - **EW1's, EW2's and the held connection's `0A000`,** rebuilt as the last round described them: as written, EW1's B2 read after the whole rollback finds 2 unmoved and the next attempt answers unnamed, and with the read made after a rollback only to the savepoint it reads 1 in the snapshot, against I10; EW2's held confirming run reads nothing and leaves its *clear* due under 2, and with the request reading, 1 is applied, against I10; the held `0A000` returns the original error, its *clear* taken with no read. EV2's, the same with a REPEATABLE READ transaction of morm's: no read and the *clear* due; with the read, against I10.
  - **EW4's,** all six forms as the last round gave them: as written, the landed *clear* under 1 and the *replace* at once under 2, with and without a pending entry; with `finish_take` under the current `<n>`, the *clear* under 2 and the *replace* held back; with the review's order, as written with the entry and held back without it.
  - **The earlier rounds' traces, rebuilt, each as before:** ET1's (one action at the read that moved `<n>` and the late request due; with the carried `<n>`, a second action, against I1); EU1's eight requests at once (one action; with no lock, eight, against I1); eight checkouts after a window with a *clear* due (one action; with a plain read and write, eight, against I1); the ticket order's, in both modes (the move kept; with no ticket, `<n>` set back to 1, against I10); the lowering's (the last-read `<n>` 3; with an overlapping greater read counting, 5, against I10); a migration seen only by an overlapping read (adopted at the next read that overlaps none; counting the overlap, at once); two standbys 0.6 s apart, straight to Postgres (one move and one action through the closed window; with the one-server order, `<n>` goes down, against I10); a no-retry transaction checked out before another call's confirmation took *clear* (its connection clears its caches and it answers; with the generation check skipped, the stale copy, against I8); a lowered `<n>` adopted behind PgBouncer, then raised back (the marker `g3.<salt>`; with no fresh salt, `g3` and its stale server statement, against I8); a confirming run that times out behind PgBouncer (the entry stays and the next no-retry transaction answers; removing it, a cached run while the entry should last, against I8); a move of `<n>` between attempts with two stale statements (one `SchemaChanged`, then both right, unnamed; running cached, against I7); a serialization failure after a `SchemaChanged`, then a kept swap by hand, entries off (the third attempt answers, unnamed; with only the attempt right after unnamed, a cached copy, against I7); a stale statement and a `%ROWTYPE` function after one column change (`.retry(1)` gets `SchemaChanged` twice and returns it, `.retry(2)` answers on its third attempt on a new connection); four replacements under a role limit at the pool's size (5 opens; with the extra tried after `53300`, 7, against I4 per epoch); EV1's, behind PgBouncer (the salt set before the raise; with the compare-and-swap raising, a cached run of the old text after the salt, against I8); with no lock, two, against I1).
