- **Each fault switch is caught,** out of 256, by the invariant T-0319 names, in the mode and at a setting of the model's own, the defaults where none is given, with the shrunk sequence's length in events. Each shrunk sequence, run alone, fails the named invariant with its fault and passes with none; the shrinking stopped after 600 runs for the longest ones, so those are short, not shortest. With each switch's own setting and no fault, no sequence fails (above).
  - **The design's machines:** no gate, 256 (4 events) and 256 (4 events) straight and behind PgBouncer, I1; no epoch, 136 (14 events) straight, I4; `42804` and `XX000` ungated, 256 (4 events) straight, I1; an epoch that never ends, 211 (5 events) straight, I8; no replacement, 256 (3 events) straight, I8; a deferred request dropped, 256 (10 events) and 256 (5 events) straight and behind PgBouncer, I2; no fresh run inside the window, 256 (6 events) and 256 (10 events) straight and behind PgBouncer, I7; a request judged by the `<n>` its call carried, 138 (80 events) and 128 (32 events) straight and behind PgBouncer, I1.
  - **The read orders:** reads applied as they arrive, 189 (4 events) and 103 (102 events) straight and behind PgBouncer, I10; `<n>` only rising in a one-server pool, 249 (3 events) and 242 (6 events) straight and behind PgBouncer, I10; reads ordered by when they were sent, 151 (81 events) straight, I10; the horizon set only by a moving read, 74 (71 events) straight, I10; an overlapping read counting when greater, 214 (9 events) straight, I10; a several-server pool following the one-server order, 256 (3 events) and 252 (4 events) straight and behind PgBouncer, I10; EV2's, a request in an open transaction reading `<n>`, 43 (595 events) straight, I10; EW1's, a row B or D read after a rollback only to the savepoint, 59 (195 events) straight, I10; EW2's, a request on a held connection reading `<n>`, 1 (1,774 events) straight, I10.
  - **The stale lock:** no stale lock, 129 (10 events) straight, I1 or I3; `old` in its own step, 227 (4 events) straight, I3; a checkout's plain read and write, 224 (5 events) and 232 (7 events) straight and behind PgBouncer, I1; no starting bit, 121 (22 events) straight, I1; a plain store into the word, 256 (9 events) behind PgBouncer, I2; EV1's, a checkout's compare-and-swap raising the generation, 253 (15 events) behind PgBouncer, I8; two momentary extras, 256 (7 events) straight, I5.
  - **The stale-copy rules:** a cached run skipping the generation check, 149 (6 events) and 216 (8 events) straight and behind PgBouncer, I8; no fresh salt on a non-rising adoption, 218 (8 events) behind PgBouncer, I8; a confirming run's outcome removing the entry, 253 (30 events) behind PgBouncer, I8.
  - **The retry and slot rules:** an attempt after `SchemaChanged` running cached statements, 256 (1 event) and 256 (2 events) straight and behind PgBouncer, I7; only the attempt right after `SchemaChanged` unnamed, 253 (2 events) and 256 (11 events) straight and behind PgBouncer, I7; a `53300` leaving the epoch's later replacements trying the extra, 209 (9 events) straight, I4.
  - **The new switch,** the savepoint bit dropped, is caught in 256 of 256 at the setting T-0319 now names for it.
  - **EW2's switch, below the floor in this model:** a request on a held connection reading `<n>` inside the app's transaction was caught in 1 of 256, and in at most 2 of 256 at every setting tried, though its trace catches it every time. The chain it needs, an app transaction's snapshot, a migration, an applied read, then a pending entry made after that read and a confirming run on the held connection with the window closed, is rare here, because this model's migrations reset cached plans and every applied move removes the entries. The nineteenth round's model caught it in 70 of 256 at the setting T-0319 names. EV2's switch, the same chain in a transaction of morm's, was caught in 43 of 256, at a setting with transactions of up to five statements. **Settled since by the twentieth review's EX1 (below):** I10's snapshot clause catches it.
- **The twentieth review, EX1 to EX6** (`reviews/step-55-review-20.md`). It found the nineteenth round and the B4 answer written in, two should-fix findings and four minor ones, none needing an owner choice. Each is fixed as the review recommends, in R-0981 and R-1270 (step 14), R-1271 and T-0319 (step 20), and R-1293's loud notice (step 50):
  - **EX1:** T-0319's I10 gains its snapshot clause: every read of `<n>` the pool applies ran on a snapshot taken after it was sent, never an open REPEATABLE READ or SERIALIZABLE transaction's, never one kept after a rollback only to a savepoint, never a held connection's app transaction's. EV2's, EW1's and EW2's switches name it. EW2's setting is stated in full, and so is the load every setting runs on. The floor sentence now states the measurement behind it, this round's model below, and the step's PR measures its own simulation's rates over 2,048 sequences, a fault under 240 of them stopping the step for the owner. Four settings the model found too weak as written are stated to what catches, below.
  - **EX2:** R-1293's notice says that on a held connection `Error::SchemaChanged`, or an `XX000` `cache lookup failed for type`, a `42804` or a `22P02` holding `_morm_retired_` after a schema change, means stale compiled code in that session: roll back, release the connection, take a new one and set its session state up again, because the same call on the same connection fails the same way; on `0A000` or any other `22P02`, roll back and run again on the same connection, because morm has dropped the stale statement. What you give up gains the connection's session state. R-1271's held-connection text, now a bullet of its own, says the same.
  - **EX3:** R-1270's step-start rule covers begun takes, a checkout's whose compare-and-swap landed during the step included; a take a step makes for its own request uses the last-read `<n>` as it stands then, after any move the step made.
  - **EX4:** R-1271's held-connection bullet states row A4 for every held connection, `Error::SchemaChanged` at each call on an old one until release, from `t0 + W` on too, and the stale-session bullet's `t0 + W` bound names it; T-0319's I8 excepts held connections, checking that a held connection's stale session ends at its release.
  - **EX5:** a `stale_faults` file, `<fault>-<mode>.events`, holds the shrunk event list itself, every random choice written out, which the test parses and feeds straight to the simulation's driver with no strategy, so a change to the generator alone leaves it as it is; the main test's proptest regression file keeps a seed, as proptest writes it.
  - **EX6:** R-0981 says a migration first seen inside a savepoint is learned at the next read of `<n>` outside a transaction that counts, and the next session-class failure after it starts its epoch.
  - **This round's own choices,** for the owner to approve through PR #3 (156 to 164 there).
  - **Raised for the owner:** nothing.
- **Postgres 17.11 (the local build at `/opt/pgsql-17`, not built from a tarball this round) and 18.6 (the Manjaro package `18.6-1`),** for EX2: throwaway clusters under the worktree's `.claude/scratch/`, Unix sockets only (`listen_addresses = ''`), the sockets under `~/.claude/scratch/`, `TMPDIR` in the scratch. A probe with asyncpg 0.31.0 for the functions and psql 18.6's `\parse`, `\bind_named` and `\close_prepared` for the prepared statement. Both versions gave the same results:
  - **A stale function stays on its session.** Session A compiled a plpgsql `DECLARE v st` function and a `%ROWTYPE` one; another session renamed `st`, created a new `st`, dropped the old one and changed the `%ROWTYPE` table's column to `bigint`. In A's own REPEATABLE READ transaction the first function got `XX000` `cache lookup failed for type 16385` and the second `42804` `type of parameter 3 (bigint) does not match that when preparing the plan (integer)`; after `ROLLBACK`, in a second such transaction, the same errors again; and again outside any transaction. A new session's calls answered.
  - **The relabelled kind, as a cutover leaves it:** after another session renamed `st` to `_morm_retired_m2_st`, renamed its labels to `_morm_retired_a` and `_morm_retired_b` and created a new `st`, a session that had compiled the function got `22P02` `invalid input value for enum _morm_retired_m2_st: "a"` in two REPEATABLE READ transactions in turn and outside one; a new session's call answered.
  - **A stale statement heals once it's dropped.** A session prepared `SELECT c FROM tp` and ran it; another changed `tp.c` to `bigint`; the prepared statement got `0A000` `cached plan must not change result type` in two REPEATABLE READ transactions in turn; once closed and the same text prepared again, it answered.
  - **Not run:** PgBouncer, because nothing this round rests on it, and Postgres 15 and 16, which T-0311 and T-0312 cover. The servers are stopped and the scratch deleted.
- **The concurrent model, rebuilt** (the last round's went with its scratch), in Python, in the worktree's `.claude/scratch/` with `TMPDIR` there, deleted after, from R-1270's procedure as now written, without reading the earlier rounds' models.
  - **How it runs.** Each call is a generator and the code between two yields is one step, so steps never interleave, as the stale lock makes them; each I/O is a yield to a discrete-event scheduler: a read of `<n>`, a run, an open, a rollback, a lock wait. Lock-free work runs inline: a checkout's read and compare-and-swap, the pending check and lookup, a prepare's read of the marker. A checkout that wins the compare-and-swap waits its lock wait, then runs `finish_take` as a step; a step's compare-and-swap that finds a due action's window ended has a checkout's land first at the setting's rate (at `take`, `set_due`, `on_moved`'s removal and an entry's creation), the step then finishing that take under the last-read `<n>` it started with and judging again.
  - **The load** is T-0319's, as now stated there, and each setting and fault switch is T-0319's as now stated, with no other default.
  - **Statements:** an enum cast touching no table, a statement whose result type a column change alters, a plpgsql `DECLARE v st` function, a `%ROWTYPE` function in place of T-0319's record-shape function, and a function converting stored text, each compiled per session at its first call. Postgres's side: a swap dropping the type leaves a cached cast `XX000` and a compiled function `XX000`; one keeping the type without a relabel gives a cast a label only the new type has `22P02` not naming the cast's type and leaves shared labels silently on the old order; a migration's swap ends with the reset, so cached plans are planned again, and relabels, so a function it didn't refresh gives `22P02` holding `_morm_retired_`; a column change gives a cached statement `0A000` and a `%ROWTYPE` function `42804`; a refresh recompiles every function.
  - **Connections:** straight to Postgres a connection is its session, and a replacement beside the old one runs the call before it takes the slot; behind PgBouncer three server sessions hold statements by text, a client's prepare of a text getting the server statement held for it. The oracle is a fresh session with a fresh statement at the run's own instant; a run in flight while a change commits is left out.
  - **The invariants** are T-0319's as now written: I1 and I3 with pairs excused one for one by a change of `<n>` after the snapshot of the read the `<n>` they were taken under came from; I2 at the first checkout after a window with a request owed; I4 per epoch, per *replace* action and over every span of `stale_cooldown`; I5; I7's clauses, held connections in the app's own transaction apart; I8 outside a transaction, inside a savepoint and under `.retry(2)`, held connections apart, the no-retry clause behind PgBouncer (at most one entry-making failure per statement per `stale_pending_expiry` with no action, move or change between) and the clearing clause (no cached run uses a copy a change left stale once an action or adoption came after that change); I10's three clauses and its new snapshot clause; I11.
  - **The settings,** 256 sequences each: the defaults, `servers = "several"` with one server, two servers, pending entries off, caps of 1, a short `stale_pending_expiry` (0.2 s), heavy stress, savepoints, a checkout landing at every eligible compare-and-swap, the caches off and no client input, each in both modes; and straight to Postgres held connections in the app's transactions (30% of calls held, 80% of those in the app's transaction), a role limit at the pool's size, a budget of eight, savepoints at READ COMMITTED, REPEATABLE READ and SERIALIZABLE only, savepoints under stress, with a role limit, with two servers and with entries off, held connections with a role limit, with two servers and under stress, and stress under a role limit: 36; and each fault switch's own setting with no fault, 42 in both modes: 78 in all.
  - **Left out:** several processes behind one PgBouncer, a per-transaction `search_path` statement, several pools in one process, a standby's recovery conflict, the transient `XX000`, a pool whose start read fails, pipelined statements, savepoints nested more than one deep and savepoints an app opens on a held connection, standbys' schema (they replay `<n>` only), I6 (structural here), I9's time bounds, and memory orderings, which T-0318's `loom` case covers.
- **Random sequences, the procedure as written.** No sequence fails any invariant in any of the 78 settings, I10's snapshot clause included. Each base setting ran 737,000 to 874,000 calls and 304,000 to 664,000 transactions, half their attempts at REPEATABLE READ or SERIALIZABLE, and 114,000 to 1,045,000 statements inside a savepoint. Straight to Postgres, at the defaults, 7,900 pool-wide actions and 10,400 epochs (20,200 and 28,000 under stress); behind PgBouncer 4,000 actions and 4,200 adoptions (6,900 and 12,600 under stress). In the savepoints setting straight to Postgres 89,300 errors came back as they are inside a savepoint, 5,400 of them a stale session's, every one before `t0 + W`, and the outer closure caught 44,500 and committed. In the held setting 201,000 held calls ran inside the app's own transaction, and no read of `<n>` was applied from its snapshot. Up to 2,300 checkouts per setting landed inside a step's compare-and-swap, up to 181 inside a step that moved `<n>`.
  - **Model corrections, not plan findings,** each found by a no-fault run failing and fixed to the procedure or the invariant as written: I1's excuse first used the moved read's snapshot for a take `finish_take` made under the step's starting `<n>` (EW4's case), where the invariant names the read that `<n>` came from; the clearing clause first looked only at the latest change before the run, not the change that left that copy stale; the momentary extra was first given back before the new connection's run, where R-0981 has the new connection take the slot after it; and the no-retry clause first counted an entry's refresh against the next entry's creation, where R-1271 bounds one failure per entry's `stale_pending_expiry`.

With each switch's own setting and no fault, no sequence fails (above).
  - **EW2's switch, the question the last round left:** with I10's snapshot clause it's caught in 72 of 256 and 67 per 256 over 2,048, where a lowered `<n>` alone caught it in 1 of 256 in the last round's model; every catch is the held connection's request read applied from the app transaction's older snapshot, whatever `<n>` it found.

- **The twenty-first review, EY1 to EY3** (`reviews/step-55-review-21.md`). It found the twentieth round written in, two should-fix findings and a minor one. EY2 needed an owner choice, and the owner chose option (a), state it and steer users to the handled form (INDEX's Decisions, "A hand-made enum swap without relabel is stated, not cured (EY2)"). EY1 and EY3 are fixed as the review recommends, with the one correction to EY1's wording below:
  - **EY1:** T-0319's I8 checks held connections as R-1271's bullet on them states, where it had only checked that a held session ends at its release, which R-0698's close on release makes true of any machine. Inside a transaction the app opened, a stale session's `XX000` from a cached statement comes back as it is at each call that meets it, at any time, with no epoch test. Apart from that, from the last change, or the end of the last refusal, plus `stale_cooldown`, a call on a held connection that meets a stale session never returns its visible error as it is, but `Error::SchemaChanged`, and before then that visible error comes back as it is only on a connection the epoch test found not old (the twenty-second review's EZ1 corrected this sentence, which had the exception in its first half only); and at release a connection row A4 marked counts as its epoch's replacement, in I4. **The correction:** the review bounded that `XX000` at once per statement per connection, and the procedure as written doesn't. Row A1's fresh run inside the app's failed transaction gets `25P02`, so the original error comes back, and the statement, evicted, is prepared again by the next call after the app's `ROLLBACK`. It's a cached statement again, so the same `XX000` comes back at each call that meets it until release. R-1271's held bullet already states that case with no count, and R-1293 tells the app to release on it. The model, checking the review's bound with no fault, fails it in 116 of 256 sequences at the defaults. So I8 says "at each call that meets it". T-0319 gains a 32nd fault switch, row A4 returning the error as it is, at the defaults straight to Postgres, so CI shows the clause can fail.
  - **EY2, option (a):**
    - **R-1271** gets a bullet of its own, "A kept type's stale function". After a swap made outside morm that keeps the old type without renaming its labels, plpgsql compiled before it, given a label only the new type has, fails with `22P02` naming the kept type, as it is, until its session ends: straight to Postgres until the connection is replaced for another reason, on a held connection until its release, behind PgBouncer until `RECONNECT` or `L`. The stale-session bullet names it, with row A4, as one of the two cases apart from its `t0 + W` bound. The held bullet's release list adds a `22P02` naming an enum type other than the one the call's value is for, and the silent-answers bullet points to the loud half.
    - **R-1293's notice** lists that `22P02` among a held connection's errors that last until release, and adds it to the release branch.
    - **R-1294, new:** the loud README notice beside R-1253's on changes made outside morm. It says what happens, when it's right (a swap that has to keep the old type's labels as they are), what you give up (those calls fail until each session ends, and morm ends none), and how to proceed: rename the kept type and its labels as morm's cutover does, or end the sessions after the swap, or make the swap a migration.
    - **T-0319:** I8 excepts the case, I9 gains its loud half (a visible such error comes only from plpgsql compiled before such a swap, never from a session opened since, or, in the app's own transaction on a held connection, from a statement cached before it; from a statement that casts no parameter to a non-built-in type it makes no request, pending entry or replacement; it ends when its session ends; worded again by EZ2, below), the row-B unit test asserts it's no signal, and a 33rd fault switch widens the session class to every such `22P02`, as option (b) would, caught by I9's loud half.
    - **T-0311** asserts the case on Postgres 15–18: outside a transaction and on a held connection, it returns as it is with the same backend pid, nothing opened, read or requested, and once the kept type and its labels are renamed, the next call's `22P02` names `_morm_retired_` and its connection is replaced.
  - **EY3:** the PR body's EX1 bullet now says five settings were restated and one invariant name widened, matching choice 159.
  - **This round's own choices,** for the owner to approve through PR #3 (165 to 172 there).
  - **Raised for the owner:** nothing.
- **Postgres 17.11 (`/opt/pgsql-17`) and 18.6 (the Manjaro package `18.6-1`),** for EY2. Throwaway clusters under the worktree's `.claude/scratch/`, Unix sockets only (`listen_addresses = ''`), the sockets under `~/.claude/scratch/`, `TMPDIR` in the scratch, probed with asyncpg 0.31.0. Both versions gave the same results:
  - **A kept type's stale function stays on its session.** Session A compiled `f(t text)`, a plpgsql function with `DECLARE v st` that assigns `t` to `v`, through a prepared `SELECT f($1::text)` given `a`. Session B ran `ALTER TYPE st RENAME TO st_old; CREATE TYPE st AS ENUM ('a','b','c')`. A's `f('c')` then got `22P02`, routine `enum_in`, `invalid input value for enum st_old: "c"`, the same way four times: outside a transaction, in a REPEATABLE READ transaction, in a second one after `ROLLBACK`, and outside again, on the same backend pid. Its `f('a')` answered `a`. A new session's `f('c')` answered `c`, and its `f('zzz')` got `22P02` naming `st`, the function's own type. So a client's bad label names the type the value is for, and the stale function's error names the kept one.
  - **Renaming afterwards turns it into the handled case.** B renamed `st_old` to `_morm_retired_m2_st` and its labels to `_morm_retired_a` and `_morm_retired_b`. A's `f('c')` then got `invalid input value for enum _morm_retired_m2_st: "c"`, outside a transaction and in a REPEATABLE READ transaction, and `f('a')` the same error for `"a"`. The new session's `f('c')` answered. So R-1294's first way to proceed works on sessions already holding the function, with no reconnect: the error now holds `_morm_retired_`, which R-1245 counts as a stale session.
  - **Not run:** PgBouncer, because nothing this round rests on it, and Postgres 15 and 16, which T-0311 covers. The servers are stopped and the scratch deleted.
- **A focused model, built for this round** in Python, in the worktree's `.claude/scratch/` with `TMPDIR` there, deleted after, from R-1270's procedure as now written. The earlier rounds' models went with their scratch. It checks what EY1 and EY2 rest on, not the whole of T-0319.
  - **How it runs.** Each call is a generator, and the code between two yields is one step, so steps never interleave, as the stale lock makes them. Each I/O is a yield to a discrete-event scheduler: a read of `<n>`, a run, an open, a rollback. Reads take tickets and apply by R-1270's read order, each on a snapshot at a random point in flight. A checkout takes a due action in one step: the starting bit and the lock wait are left out, because nothing this round touches them.
  - **The load** is T-0319's, apart from savepoints, which are left out: 2,000 events, a call about every 20 ms with a fifth in bursts, the five statements below equally, eight values, the label mix, 35% `.retry(0)` to `.retry(2)` transactions, straight to Postgres 10% of calls on held connections (half in the app's own transaction, which a stale error fails, so the fresh run and the read get `25P02`), world events at 5.5% of events in T-0319's mix, and its I/O lengths and failure rates. After an error in its own transaction the app rolls back and runs the statement again half the time, which T-0319 didn't state until the twenty-second review's EZ4.
  - **Statements:** an enum cast touching no table, a statement whose result type a column change alters, a plpgsql `DECLARE v st` function called through `SELECT f($1::text)`, a function converting stored text, and a `%ROWTYPE` function, each compiled per session at its first call and again after a refresh. Postgres's side is as last round's model had it, plus the loud half: a kept swap without a relabel gives that function, compiled before it, `22P02` naming the kept type for a label only the new type has, as the probe above showed. A migration relabels only the type it retires, so a type kept by hand earlier stays as it was.
  - **Behind PgBouncer:** three server sessions, each holding compiled functions and up to 16 server statements by text, the text carrying the marker. A transaction reaches its client connection's last session or, 30% of the time, another. Sessions are replaced after 4 s, and all at once after a migration's `RECONNECT`, half the time. Rows C1, C2, D1 and D2 are built with the cool-down, the salt and `on_moved`'s adoption.
  - **What it checks:** I4 per epoch, a held connection row A4 marked counted at its release; I5; I7 outside a transaction (on a held connection outside the app's own transaction too) and under `.retry(2)`; I8 as now written, its held clause included, and behind PgBouncer that no stale session is visible on a server session opened since the change; and I9's loud half as now written. The oracle is a fresh session with a fresh statement at the run's own instant, compared by SQLSTATE. A run in flight while a change commits is left out, as before.
  - **Left out:** savepoints, I1 to I3, I6, I10 and I11, I4's span bound, the starting bit and the lock wait, role limits and `53300`, several servers, several pools or processes, the per-transaction `search_path` statement, the record-shape function (the `%ROWTYPE` one stands in), a standby's recovery conflict, the transient `XX000`, a failing start read, and the settings other than those below. None of them is a path EY1 or EY2 changes, and the twentieth round's model ran them against the same procedure.
- **Random sequences, the procedure as written,** 256 sequences each, seeds 0 to 255. No sequence fails any invariant checked in any of these settings:
  - **The defaults, both modes:** straight to Postgres 873,000 calls, 306,000 transactions and 87,000 held calls (43,500 in the app's own transaction). Row A4 returned `Error::SchemaChanged` 3,868 times, and 3,136 held connections it marked counted as their epoch's replacements at release. 757 stale sessions' errors came back as they are on held connections the test found not old, all before `tc + W`. 584 stale sessions' `XX000`s from a cached statement came back as they are in the app's own transaction, 173 of them a second time or more on the same connection and statement. 861 errors were the kept type's stale function's, 149 of them on held connections. Behind PgBouncer: 872,000 calls and 4,450 of the kept type's errors.
  - **Held connections in the app's transactions** (30% of calls held, 80% of those in the app's own transaction): 260,000 held calls, 208,500 inside the app's transaction, 10,418 `Error::SchemaChanged` from row A4 and 7,742 releases counted.
  - **Quiet,** to give `tc + W` room: world events at 1% of events, kept swaps 4 in 9 of them, migrations 2 in 9, and column changes, drops and stored text toggled 1 in 9 each. Straight to Postgres 1,055 of the kept type's errors at the defaults' held share and 219 at the held setting's; behind PgBouncer 6,943.
- **EY2's case is in the model, and I8 needs its exception.** With I8's exception for the kept type's stale function taken out, the quiet setting fails in 53 of 256 sequences straight to Postgres, each by a kept type's `22P02` returned as it is from `tc + W` on. Behind PgBouncer I8 needs no exception: its clause there is about server sessions opened since the change, which the kept type's error can't come from. 253 of 256 sequences there have such an error from `tc + W` on, a count of how often the case comes up (EZ5). At the defaults, where changes rarely leave `stale_cooldown` free of one, it fails 2 of 256. With the exception, as written, none fails.
- **EY1's clause can fail.** Row A4 returning a stale session's error as it is, run as a fault form, fails I8's clause on held connections in 236 of 256 sequences at the defaults straight to Postgres, 235 per 256 over 2,048 more (seeds 1,000 to 3,047), and 241 of 256 at the held setting. Every catch is the clause's second part: the error came back as it is on a connection the test found old. The first part alone, from `tc + W` on, catches it in 0 of 256 at the defaults and at the quiet held setting, because held connections are closed at release, and old pooled connections holding a stale session are replaced before `tc + W`. So the clause as a whole is what CI's 32nd switch needs, and T-0319 names it whole.
- **The widened signal is caught.** Every `22P02` from `enum_in` that no other row takes, counted as session class as option (b) would, run as a fault form: I9's loud half fails in 222 of 256 sequences at the defaults straight to Postgres, and 217 per 256 over 2,048 more (seeds 1,000 to 3,047), each by a kept type's error getting a replacement, a request or a pending entry. Behind PgBouncer it fails in 255 of 256, each by a retry on another server session or a cool-down entry.
- **The review's once-per-statement bound,** checked with no fault at the defaults, fails in 116 of 256 sequences, as the correction above says.
- **The twenty-second review, EZ1 to EZ6** (`reviews/step-55-review-22.md`). It found the twenty-first round written in, three should-fix findings and three minor ones, none needing an owner choice. Each is fixed as the review recommends, with the corrections below. Because the last two rounds each fixed a sentence and left its neighbour wrong, this round also wrote every invariant, I1 to I11, and R-1271's bullets as literal checks in a model, read every "only", "never", "at most" and "from then on" against R-1270's procedure, and fixed the ones that fail with nothing broken:
  - **EZ1:** T-0319's I8 puts the app's own transaction's `XX000` first, over both halves, at any time and with no epoch test, and limits both halves to visible errors; R-1271's held bullet, R-1293, the PR body and the EY1 entry above say it the same way. **The correction to the review's sentence:** with its exception as recommended, the clause still fails with no fault, because a call whose own bound value holds `_morm_retired_` gets its `22P02` as it is by R-0981's parameter test, with no epoch test, even when a stale session raised it and a fresh one would answer: the value needn't be the one converted. The model, where T-0319's load binds a label to every statement, fails the recommended sentence's half before then in 6 of 256 at the load and 26 of 256 with long holds, and its half from then on in 1 of 256 with long holds. So the parameter test joins the kept type's function as a case apart, at any time, held or not, in I8 and in R-1271's bullet on stale sessions.
  - **EZ2:** I9's loud half is limited to visible errors and names the cast in the app's own transaction, at most once per copy. **Beside it:** the sentence "it makes no request, pending entry or replacement" spoke of an error "returned as it is", so the 33rd switch, which replaces the connection and answers, would have slipped past a literal check: it's now a run that ends in such an error, always returned as it is with nothing requested, entered, retried, discarded or replaced for it. It says too that such an error can be the outcome of another signal's fresh or confirming run, or of its retry, which counts as any server error does: the literal sentence failed 11 of 256 at the load straight to Postgres and 92 behind PgBouncer.
  - **EZ3:** R-1294 gives both ways to proceed, ending the sessions first, which always works, and the rename only once nothing in use is on the kept type, with what breaks otherwise, a name no migration has (`_morm_retired_byhand_st`) and what `import`, `verify` and `check_schema` do with it. **Beside it:** the rename alone isn't what morm's cutover does, which ends with the reset, and the probe below shows that without it a function or a cached statement holding a constant label answers with `_morm_retired_a` and no error. So the notice gives the reset too, in the same transaction, R-1271's bullet and its second silent case say it, R-1253's second silent case says it, and T-0311 asserts it with and without.
  - **EZ4:** T-0319's load states the app's second run, in a new transaction of its own. **Beside it,** what the load left open or didn't reach: the label only the newest type has is the newest when the statement runs, which the 17th switch, a held connection's request reading `<n>`, rests on (caught in 73 of 256 so, and in 0 with the label fixed when the call is drawn); the paths of the statement under a `search_path` and the shapes of the record-shape call; a setting with held connections kept longer, without which I8's half from then on is reached in 1 of 256 sequences with the 32nd switch, and no stale `XX000` from `tc + W` on is met in the app's own transaction (295 with it); and a setting with two processes behind one PgBouncer.
  - **EZ5:** the twenty-first round's two sentences on its model are corrected above and in the PR body.
  - **EZ6:** R-1270's `on_error` limits the parameter test to a `22P02` holding `_morm_retired_`, as R-0981, R-1245's table and T-0153 have it, and T-0153 asserts that a `0A000` of a call binding such a value gets its fresh run. With the line as it was, the model fails I7 outside a transaction in 89 of 256 sequences straight to Postgres and 167 behind PgBouncer.
  - **The other clauses that failed with no fault, read literally,** out of 256 sequences at the load, straight to Postgres and behind PgBouncer, or where said:
    - I1 spoke of the read the last-read `<n>` came from, which a begun take finished after its step's own move isn't judged by: 34, and in a pool that reaches several servers, "rose between them", 118 to 357 of 2,048. It's now the read the `<n>` the first was taken under came from, and a greater `<n>` for the second.
    - I2 left out an adoption taking a deferred request's place: 256 behind PgBouncer.
    - I8's "never visible after `RECONNECT` or `server_lifetime`" left out a call or a transaction already running, which keeps its server session: 171 and 2 behind PgBouncer. It's about calls and attempts begun since; R-1271's bullet says so too.
    - I8's "at most once per `stale_pending_expiry`" for a stale statement behind PgBouncer left out calls already running, entries off, the caps and what ends an entry sooner: 101, and 255 where no transaction retries; for a `search_path` switch it left out a moved `<n>`, the caps and entries off: 30 and 154. - **Postgres 17.11 (`/opt/pgsql-17`) and 18.6 (the Manjaro package `18.6-1`),** throwaway clusters under the worktree's `.claude/scratch/`, Unix sockets only (`listen_addresses = ''`) under `~/.claude/scratch/`, `TMPDIR` in the scratch, probed with asyncpg 0.31.0, each cached statement a named prepared one. "The reset" is `contracts.md`'s operator made and dropped, in `public`. Both versions gave the same results:
  - **EZ3, the rename and the reset.** A session compiled `k()`, `DECLARE v st; v := 'a'`, and prepared `SELECT ('a'::st < 'b'::st)::text || ('a'::st)::text`, each run seven times; another renamed `st` to `st_old` and made `st` with another order. After the rename to `_morm_retired_byhand_st` with its labels and no reset, `k()` answered `_morm_retired_a` and the statement `true_morm_retired_a`, with no error, where a new session gave `a` and `falsea`. With the reset in the same transaction, `k()` got `22P02` naming `_morm_retired_byhand_st` and the statement gave `falsea`. A function converting its argument got that `22P02` either way.
  - **EZ3, something still on the kept type.** A column on it read `a` before the rename and `_morm_retired_a` after, through a view over it and for a row its own default wrote too; `WHERE c = 'a'` and `INSERT ... VALUES ('a')` got `22P02` naming `_morm_retired_byhand_st` in a new session, and `'a'::st_old` `42704`.
  - **EZ2.** The stale function given `'zzz'` got `22P02` naming `st_old`. A cast cached before the swap, given `'c'` inside `BEGIN`, got the same; a fresh prepare there got `25P02`, and the text prepared again after `ROLLBACK` answered. After `st_old` was dropped, the function got `XX000` in three transactions in turn and once outside.
  - **The parameter test.** After a cutover's swap with the reset, a session's function that assigns a constant and returns its text argument, given `_morm_retired_x`, got `22P02` `invalid input value for enum _morm_retired_m2_st: "a"`; a new session answered. So R-0981's test returns a stale session's error to a call whose value has nothing to do with it.
  - **The third silent case.** After the same swap, a session's stale `f` got `22P02` for `a`, `c` and `_morm_retired_x`, and answered `_morm_retired_a` for `_morm_retired_a`, where a new session got `22P02` naming `st`.
  - **A statement that's stale and meets a stale session.** A cached `SELECT c, g(id) FROM t WHERE id = $1`, with `g` holding a `t%ROWTYPE` variable, got `0A000` from `RevalidateCachedQuery` after `ALTER TABLE t ALTER c TYPE bigint`, its fresh run `42804` from `plpgsql_param_eval_recfield`, and a new session answered. And a cached `INSERT` that casts a parameter to `st`, with a trigger compiled before a cutover that converts a text column, got `22P02` naming `_morm_retired_m2_st`, cached and fresh alike: both R-1245's second and seventh rows match it.
  - **R-1245's second row.** A cast cached before a kept swap, given the new label `in st`, got `invalid input value for enum st_old2: "in st"`, which holds `st` as a whole word.
  - **Not run:** PgBouncer, because nothing this round rests on it beyond R-1258's "each server connection closes once its transaction ends", and Postgres 15 and 16, which T-0151 to T-0153 and T-0311 cover. The servers are stopped and the scratch deleted.
- **A concurrent model, built again** (the earlier rounds' went with their scratch), in Rust, about 3,200 lines with no crate, from R-1270's procedure and T-0319 as now written, without any earlier model's code. Deleted with the scratch.
  - **How it runs.** Each call is an async function polled by a discrete-event scheduler, and the code between two awaits is one step. Each I/O is an await: a read of `<n>` with its ticket and its snapshot at a random point in flight, a run, an open, a rollback, a lock wait. A checkout that wins its compare-and-swap waits its lock wait, then runs `finish_take` as a step; a step's compare-and-swap that finds a due action's window ended has a checkout's land first half the time.
  - **The load** is T-0319's, as now stated: the six statements, savepoints, held connections with the second run, `.retry(0)` to `.retry(2)`, REPEATABLE READ and SERIALIZABLE attempts, migrations, lowerings, hand-made swaps and column changes, refused opens, stored text, and behind PgBouncer three server sessions holding 16 statements each.
  - **What it checks:** I1 to I6, I7, I8 with its held, savepoint, retry, per-entry, `RECONNECT`, `server_lifetime` and clearing clauses, I9 with both halves and the time of a cached statement's, I10 with its snapshot clause, I11, and R-1271's "before `t0 + W`", "from `t0 + W` on", "each connection's stale copy fails at most once" and catalog reads per call. Each changed sentence is written three ways where they differ: as `93854c2` had it, as the review recommends, and as it now stands. The oracle is a fresh session with a fresh statement at the run's own instant, compared by SQLSTATE.
  - **The settings, no fault,** 256 sequences of 2,000 events each (seeds 0 to 255) and 2,048 more (seeds 1,000 to 3,047): the defaults, entries off, caps of 1, `stale_cooldown` five times `stale_pending_expiry`, a budget of eight, the caches off, no client input, `servers = "several"` with one, two and three servers, world events at 20%, a checkout landing at every eligible compare-and-swap (alone, and with two servers and 20% world events), savepoints, REPEATABLE READ and SERIALIZABLE only, quiet (world events 1%), hand-made swaps renamed afterwards as R-1294 says, transactions up to 0.3 s between statements, a call every 0.8 s, and transactions with no retry, each straight to Postgres and behind PgBouncer; straight, 30% of calls held with 80% of those in the app's transaction (at the defaults and quiet), no second run, held connections kept longer (at 10%, at 30% of calls, quiet, and with the hand renames), a role limit at the pool's size (alone and with 20% world events) and refused opens at 6 of 18 world events; behind PgBouncer, two processes and three with world events at 15%. 52 settings, 30 straight and 22 behind PgBouncer, and each switch's own setting.
  - **As now written, no check fails in any of them,** in the 256, in the 2,048, or, in the 52, in 20,000 more each (seeds 100,000 to 119,999). At the load straight to Postgres, 256 sequences make 871,000 calls, 305,000 transactions and 86,600 held calls, 43,400 in the app's transaction; row A4 returned `Error::SchemaChanged` 3,639 times, 490 `XX000`s came back as they are in the app's transaction (2,232 with held connections kept longer, 295 of them from `tc + W` on), 25 casts' `22P02`s naming the kept type (55), and 805 of the kept type's errors, 135 on held connections.

- **The owner's five answers to the twenty-second fix round's open questions,** option (a) on each, written in since `19be048`:
  - **1, a fresh run that ends in a session-class error, outside a transaction, and the order of R-1245's rows.** R-1270's `on_error` sends a fresh run's session-class error on to A2 to A4 or C2 whatever the first error was, the parameter test's apart, and R-1245 says which row an error more than one row matches takes, the seventh before the second (R-0718's rows A1, C1 and D1, R-0981, R-1246's C2). R-1271 drops the stale-session bullet's fourth case and has a bullet on the case given up, a kept type renamed by hand without the reset, and R-1294 says it loudly. **Beyond what the question said:** the case is larger behind PgBouncer, where the statement's error comes back as it is from the cool-down, again and again, until `RECONNECT`, `server_lifetime`, a salt or an adoption, because row D2 salts nothing; open question 2 in the PR body.
  - **2, the random part.** R-0995 says what it is, 64 bits as 16 hex digits, one per run, recorded in the ledger and committed before the run's first step, so the cutover's attempts, `--resume` and crash recovery reuse it; who finds a label by it (the inverse, down, rollback, `verify`, fingerprints) and who only by the `_morm_retired_` start, which doesn't change (step 14's signals, the parameter test, `import`, `check_schema`); what's left of the 63 bytes, 32; and that no enum can have been retired without one before 0.1. R-1050 says how a migration file, which can't know it, writes such a label and how the text sent gets it, beside the mapping of names. R-1023, R-1049, R-0977, R-0997, R-1001, `contracts.md`, the notices R-1249, R-1253 and R-1294, R-1271's third silent case and I9 follow, with T-0309, T-0315, T-0175, T-0176 and T-0151. **What it leaves:** text copied out of the database after the cutover, from the retired column or `pg_enum`; open question 3.
  - **3, the second row's test,** replaced since by the three answers' round, below, which reads no bound text, and the name test itself since gone, by the answer to the two answers' question, last below. R-1245 then said which of the call's texts are taken out of the message and how the name is found in the rest. **Beside it,** read literally: "as a whole identifier" didn't say how Postgres prints a name, so it found `st` in `"st old"` and in `old.st`, and with other letters counted as the identifier's, never in the Japanese message `列挙型stの…`; and "that cast's type name" was wrong for a statement with two casts. **What it leaves:** a value the call didn't bind, and a type named as a word of the server's message; open question 1, since answered.
  - **4, row C2.** R-1270 has the second checkout as its own paragraph, with what it may give back, its queue, its failure, and why nothing else in the procedure waits for a second connection: A2's extra is an open decided by what's free, and its slot's reopening keeps the slot and its place in the budget; B3's and a checkout's replacement open in a slot; every read runs on its call's own connection; A4's comes at release. R-1246's row C2, R-1258, T-0306, T-0314, T-0318, R-1271's new bullet on waiting and I12 follow.
  - **5, the pending entry's `XX000` bit,** in R-1270's state table and procedure and R-0718's entry, with "the same error" said once, in R-1245, as the same SQLSTATE, which is what the three bits give; I13 checks it.

The no-stale-lock switch is split at every read and write of the state (the read order's, the request's, the take's and the epoch's) with up to 20 ms between parts: 256 of 256 and 2,048 of 2,048, by each of I1, I3 and I10's newest-snapshot clause on its own in 256, 256 and 239. With the parts up to 0.5 ms apart, as before, the whole split is caught in 144 (I1 in 91, I3 in 83). EW2's switch, the lowest before, has a setting that meets it: 254 and 2,029.

- **Postgres 17.11 (`/opt/pgsql-17`) and 18.6 (the Manjaro package `18.6-1`),** throwaway clusters under the worktree's `.claude/scratch/`, Unix sockets only under `~/.claude/scratch/`, `TMPDIR` in the scratch, probed with asyncpg 0.31.0. Both gave the same results:
  - **The random part.** After a cutover's swap whose labels were `_morm_retired_7c1e9a04b52fd386_a` and `..._b`, with the reset, a session that had compiled `f` (`DECLARE v st`) got `22P02` naming `_morm_retired_m2_st` for `a`, `c` and `_morm_retired_a`, which it used to take, and so did a function assigning a constant and one comparing two labels both types share; it took `_morm_retired_7c1e9a04b52fd386_a`, and the retired column's own text. Down's expression, with the recorded part, read the rows back.
  - **63 bytes.** The prefix is 31 bytes. A 32-byte label was renamed whole, at 63 bytes; a 33-byte one's 64 got `42602` `invalid enum label`, `Labels must be 63 bytes or less`, as a 64-byte label in `CREATE TYPE` does; two 63-byte labels sharing 40 bytes, through the name function, came out as two labels of 63 bytes; renaming one label to another's name got `42710`.
  - **How the message prints a name.** A cached cast on a kept type printed `st_old`, `"st old"`, `"my st old"`, `"St"`, `"st""x"`, `value_old`, `"名old"`, and `old.st` after `ALTER TYPE st SET SCHEMA old`; a bad label on a type off the `search_path` printed `old.st`.

  - **The message in every language Postgres ships:** `enum_in`'s `msgstr` in all 16 `.po` files of `REL_15_STABLE` to `REL_18_STABLE` (12 in 15: `de es fr it ja ka ko ru sv uk zh_CN zh_TW`; `id`, `pl`, `pt_BR` and `tr` from 16), the same in each branch, none fuzzy: the type first, no colon before the value's, then a colon, the opening mark, the value, the closing mark and nothing after. On 18.6, with `lc_messages` set, 12 of them as printed; `/opt/pgsql-17` is built without them.
  - **What the test leaves.** A cached `(c::st)::text` beside a parameter's cast, with a kept swap and `in st` in the column, got `invalid input value for enum kept: "in st"` and its fresh run answered; types named `value`, `enum` and `input` printed inside a message that holds the same word.
  - **A fresh run that meets a stale session after a `22P02`:** a cached cast beside a `%ROWTYPE` function got the cast's `22P02` after a kept swap and a column change, its fresh run the function's `42804`, and a new session answered.

The name test of answer 1, and the evidence and model checks below that served it (the messages in every release and language, the rule over them, the unreadable messages, the settings of a type named as a word and of messages the test can't read, and the breaks of the test), are replaced since by the answer to the two answers' question, last below, which reads no message text; they're kept as the record:

R-1271 and R-1253 state what's left: a server in another language and a type named as a word of its message.

R-1294 left out the pool's next salt and that a call outside a transaction gets the error too, and says both now, in bold.

- **The owner's answer to the two answers' open question,** option (a), written in since `64820fe`: **R-1245's second row takes every cached statement's `22P02` from `enum_in`, and the name test goes.** The fresh run decides each, and R-1245 reads no message text for it.
  - **How the error is recognised, in every language:** by the SQLSTATE, `22P02`, and the routine field, `enum_in`, which Postgres never translates, from a cached statement, after the eighth row (the parameter test), the ninth (the transient `XX000`) and the seventh (`_morm_retired_`, an identifier no translation changes), which go first as before. Nothing else about the message is read, so the language, the type's name, how it prints, `quote_all_identifiers`, the encoding and the release's text don't enter. R-1245's row says "whatever its message says", and its paragraph "The second row reads no message text" says why; "Nothing else takes it" keeps what the test's "Nothing else raises it" said, with the routines now named.
  - **What went with the test:** the value's mark, quote handling, how a name is compared and its schema rule, the English and other-language readings, the unreadable messages and the `LATIN2` case, "when the test isn't asked" with its "more than one non-built-in type", the 17 languages' texts in T-0318, the cases T-0151 asserted only for the test (`in st`, `x "st"`, `st: "bad`, `st old`, `value`, `a: "b`, `quote_all_identifiers`, `billing.st` off the path, the `LATIN2` database), R-1271's bullet "What the second row's test can't tell" and both stale-statement bullets' exception for it, R-1253's bullet on stale statements that reached you as their own error, and in T-0319 the language and name draws, the labels holding the type's name, three settings (a type named as a word in English and in Spanish, messages the test can't read) and the setting of a kept type moved where the `search_path` finds it, the language exceptions of I7 and of I9's loud half, and I14's "no error that names the type a cached cast is on now ... is taken". The kept-function sentence on an exchange of names that left its type under the cast's name went too: that error now takes the second row like any other.
  - **What stays:** `enum_in`'s SQLSTATE and routine as the second row's signal, the parameter test (R-0981, T-0153), which is a different test on a message holding `_morm_retired_`, and the seventh row, before the second.
  - **What's given up,** as the decision words it, in R-1271's "What the second row costs" and loud in R-1253: a single enum cast's bad label, and bad stored text any cached statement converts, cost one more run outside a transaction, and inside one an `Error::SchemaChanged` in place of the `22P02` with no `.retry(n)`, once per `stale_pending_expiry` per statement, with that statement unnamed meanwhile; the text keeps the costs the last round found the decision's words leave out (row D1's catalog read, once more after anything that ends an entry sooner, behind PgBouncer once per call whose own read found `<n>` moved, and a confirmation's request through the gate), and R-1253 says what you give up, when it's right and how to proceed.
- **Every message Postgres 15 to 18 ships, checked for anything else that takes the second row** (`probes/src8.py`, `results/src8.txt`): every C, yacc, lex and header file of 15.20, 16.16, 17.12 and 18.7, the whole tree with contrib and the procedural languages, from ftp.postgresql.org's tarballs checked by their published SHA-256, has one function named `enum_in`, in `enum.c`, and its two error sites raise `ERRCODE_INVALID_TEXT_REPRESENTATION`, `invalid input value for enum %s: "%s"`, by `ereport` on 15 and `ereturn` from 16; the only other `"enum_in"` is the function table `fmgrtab.c`. `enum.c` in all 58 release tags, `REL_15_0` to `REL_18_7`, read from GitHub, has the same two sites in every one. `check_safe_enum_use`'s `55P04` is its own routine's. So a `22P02` with routine `enum_in` is that message alone, or its re-raise by a plpgsql handler, which keeps both.
- **Postgres 17.11 and 18.6,** throwaway clusters under the worktree's `.claude/scratch/`, Unix sockets only under `~/.claude/scratch/`, `TMPDIR` in the scratch, asyncpg 0.31.0 (`probes/probe7.py`, `run7.sh`), the same on both line for line. 17.11 was built from ftp.postgresql.org's `postgresql-17.11.tar.bz2`, SHA-256 `dd27f2b3c59e73ed14aa3324901242bf69a032a6347805f274e6260322d42979`, with translations, which the last rounds' `/opt/pgsql-17` lacks; 18.6 is the Manjaro package. The probe runs the decision itself beside asyncpg, on a connection with the statement prepared before the change: outside a transaction one fresh run on the second row's signal; in a transaction with no `.retry(n)` the rollback and `Error::SchemaChanged`; under `.retry(1)` the same, then the second attempt on a statement prepared again, as morm's unnamed run; each with `lc_messages` `C`, `es_ES.utf8` and `de_DE.utf8`:
  - **The two conversion cases of the open question:** after an exchange of `st` and `kind`, a cached `SELECT (($1::text)::st)::text, (c::kind)::text FROM s WHERE id = $2` got `invalid input value for enum st: "k2"` (`la sintaxis de entrada no es válida para el enum st: «k2»`, `ungültiger Eingabewert für Enum st: »k2«`), and after `cmp` over `st` was kept and made again over `st2`, `SELECT (($1::text)::st)::text, (c::cmp)::text ...` got the same for `b`; each `22P02` with routine `enum_in`. Outside a transaction the fresh run answered (`k`, `k2` and `a`, `(b)`); with no `.retry(n)` the call got `Error::SchemaChanged`; under `.retry(1)` the second attempt answered; a new session answered.
  - **The language case:** a cast to an enum named `entrada`, a word of the Spanish message, kept by hand as `entrada_vieja` and made again with a new label `c`: `la sintaxis de entrada no es válida para el enum entrada_vieja: «c»`, and the same three outcomes as the conversion cases, in Spanish as in English and German.

  - **FC1,** one statement under a per-transaction `search_path`, `E` = `W` = 1 s, 50 transactions a second on a pool of four: straight to Postgres 2.00 `Error::SchemaChanged` per `E`, of which 1.03 began after another's error and before its entry was made, and 0.97 otherwise; behind PgBouncer 2.20, 1.20 and 1.00. That's about one per `E` plus about one more begun before the entry, the review's own model's order (1.18 and 1.34 at 1 s, 0.47 and 1.29 at the 60-second defaults).
  - **FC2,** with entries off behind PgBouncer: 3,104 stale statements' `Error::SchemaChanged` at "entries off" and 20,319 at the load of transactions with no `.retry(n)`, none from a call begun after a pool-wide action taken since its change.
  - **Not run:** Postgres and PgBouncer, because FC1 and FC2 are morm's own procedure, which the model drives, and the review's probes on 17.11 and 18.6 show the server's side; the switches, 2,048 and 20,000 sequences, and the 60-second defaults, since no check changed.
- **The twenty-sixth review's fix,** FD1 to FD3 (`reviews/step-55-review-26.md`), since `e217a1c`, each as the review recommends, read literally, with the text beside each checked for the same error. All three are wording; none changes the procedure.
  - **FD1:** the README's gap clauses count the transactions that run the statement before morm marks it pending, as R-1271 and T-0319's I8 count runs, not transactions by when they start: one begun before the first failure's error that reaches the statement before the entry is made fails too, and one begun meanwhile that reaches it after doesn't. R-1253's second bullet now says "plus the transactions that run the statement before morm has rolled back the first, checked the schema's state and marked the statement pending", and its last bullet "plus the transactions that run it before morm marks it pending"; R-1272's bound says "plus the transactions that run it before morm, after rolling back that failure and, when it needs to, checking the schema's state, marks the statement pending, a few more at a high rate", its "Each failure asks for one repair, once" "plus the transactions that run it before morm marks it pending", and "What you give up" "plus the transactions that run it before morm marks it pending, a few more at a high rate". Every other place that names these calls already counts runs: R-1271's "calls of that statement that begin before its entry is made" and "beyond calls already running when its entry was made", R-0984's "beyond calls that begin before its entry is made", and I8.
  - **FD2:** R-1268's shorter `stale_pending_expiry` says "about once per `stale_pending_expiry` per statement", as R-1253 and R-1292 do. The sweep of every bound on a bad enum value's or a stale statement's `Error::SchemaChanged` for one with neither "about" nor an exception found three more: R-1271's bullet on a kept type's stale function, "once per pending entry", now "beyond calls of that statement that begin before its entry is made"; R-1288's stale statement after a lowered `<n>`, "once", now "once per connection", the evict's bound, because that sentence is about straight to Postgres; and the second half of the bound for several statements under a per-transaction `search_path`, "or once per `E`" in R-1271 and "or once per `stale_pending_expiry`" in R-1272, now "or about once". Left as they are: the bounds with an exception (R-1271's stale-statement and second-row bullets, R-0984, I8); the per-connection bounds straight to Postgres, which the evict gives (R-0718, R-1253, R-1268, R-1271); T-0312's, whose alternations run one after another; and R-1249's behind PgBouncer, "inside one it returns `Error::SchemaChanged` once", which is exact, because row D2 puts the statement in the cool-down at the failure, before the rollback, so a call of it failing after that returns its error as it is.
  - **FD3:** the twenty-fifth round's FC1 record above, and the PR body's FC1 bullet, say what each of R-1272's three places now reads.
- **The last round's model, carried on** (Rust, kept with its scripts and outputs in `plans/models/step-55/` on `main`, where the coordinator puts each round's). FD1 changes what it counts, not what it checks: I8 already fails a failure in a run begun while its statement's entry exists, which is the set R-1253 and R-1272 now state. FD2 and FD3 change nothing it checks or counts: R-1268's and R-1292's rate is I14's, R-1271's kept function's is I14's too, and R-1288's per-connection bound is I8's. What's new (`model/fd1.sh`, `results/fd1.txt`):
  - **The FD1 counts:** each `search_path` `Error::SchemaChanged` with entries on and no `.retry(n)`, classed against the earliest earlier failure whose entry wasn't made when its run began: its run already in flight at that failure's error, its transaction begun before that error, or begun after it, in the gap; with no such failure, the first. The README's older clause, which named only the last kind, is kept as a check of an older wording, "lit FD1".
  - **One new setting,** in both modes: the `search_path` setting with up to 100 ms between a transaction's checkout and its statement's run, as when it runs other statements first. That's 82 settings, 45 straight to Postgres and 37 behind PgBouncer.
  - **As now written, no check fails in any of them** over 256 sequences of 2,000 events (seeds 0 to 255), 2,048 more (1,000 to 3,047) and 20,000 more (10,000 to 29,999); the 80 earlier settings give the same results as at `e217a1c`, with "lit FD1" added where it fails.
  - **Every switch is caught** exactly as at `018b3fb`, all 39, in 54 to 256 of 256 and 411 to 2,048 of 2,048, the floor the 11th straight to Postgres (`results/switches.txt`); breaks as before (`results/breaks.txt`).
  - **What FD1 counts,** 256 sequences of 40 seconds at 50 transactions a second on a pool of four, `E` = `W` = 1 s, per `E`:

    | Setting | All | The first | In flight | Begun before | Begun in the gap | "lit FD1" fails |
    |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
    | Straight to Postgres, run at checkout | 2.00 | 0.93 | 0.04 | 0.00 | 1.03 | 194 of 256 |
    | Straight to Postgres, up to 100 ms to the run | 1.92 | 0.93 | 0.03 | 0.57 | 0.39 | 248 of 256 |
    | Behind PgBouncer, run at checkout | 2.20 | 0.96 | 0.04 | 0.00 | 1.20 | 199 of 256 |
    | Behind PgBouncer, up to 100 ms to the run | 2.13 | 0.96 | 0.03 | 0.72 | 0.41 | 256 of 256 |

    With the run at checkout, nearly all of the extra failures begin in the gap, which is why the last round's counts, by when the run began, agreed with the older clause. With up to 100 ms to the run, more than half come from transactions begun before the earlier failure's error, the review's model's order (0.54 and 1.00 per `E` at the 60-second defaults with 1 to 100 ms); none comes from a run begun while the entry exists.
  - **Not run:** Postgres and PgBouncer, because FD1 to FD3 are wording of morm's own procedure, which the model drives, and the review's probes on 17.11 and 18.6 show the server's side; the 60-second defaults, which the review's model ran.

### TLS over a Unix socket: evidence

For the owner's `sslmode` decision, on a throwaway Postgres 18.6 (the Manjaro package `18.6-1`, a cluster made with its `initdb` under `.claude/scratch/`, with `TMPDIR` there and the socket under `~/.claude/scratch/`), with `ssl = on` and a self-signed certificate, then `ssl = off`. The driver was `tokio-postgres` 0.7.18, the version the workspace's `Cargo.lock` holds, in a throwaway probe built offline, with `NoTls` and with a connector that says it can do TLS and fails any handshake, so reaching the handshake shows TLS was tried; `psql` 18.6 stood for libpq.

| Connection | `sslmode` | `tokio-postgres` 0.7.18, `NoTls` | `tokio-postgres` 0.7.18, TLS-capable connector | `psql` 18.6 (libpq) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Unix socket, `ssl = on` | `disable` | connects, no TLS | connects, no TLS | connects, no TLS |
| Unix socket, `ssl = on` | `prefer` | connects, no TLS | connects, no TLS | connects, no TLS |
| Unix socket, `ssl = on` | `require` | **fails:** `server does not support TLS` | **fails:** `server does not support TLS` | connects, no TLS |
| Unix socket, `ssl = on` | `verify-ca`, `verify-full` | (no such mode in the driver) | (no such mode in the driver) | connect, no TLS |
| TCP, `ssl = on` | `prefer` | connects, no TLS | tries the handshake; when it fails, fails, with no fallback to plain | TLS |
| TCP, `ssl = on` | `require` | fails: `no TLS implementation configured` | tries the handshake | TLS |
| TCP, `ssl = off` | `prefer` | connects, no TLS | connects, no TLS | connects, no TLS |
| TCP, `ssl = off` | `require` | fails: `server does not support TLS` | fails: `server does not support TLS` | fails: `server does not support SSL, but SSL was required` |

So Postgres never offers TLS on a Unix socket, libpq ignores `sslmode` there, and `tokio-postgres` 0.7.18 doesn't: its `connect_tls` sends the TLS request on any stream and fails `require` when the server answers no. It also calls the connector's `make_tls_connect` with an empty host name for a socket, even with `disable`, which a rustls connector may refuse. The driver can still do what the owner decided, because the mode and the connector are morm's to choose per connection: morm gives a socket host `SslMode::Disable` and `NoTls`, and a TCP host the mode and connector `sslmode` asks for, with one driver config per host when a host list mixes the two (R-1277). Not run: Postgres 15 to 17 and a TLS handshake with a real rustls connector, which T-0323 runs on every version; the probe and the server were deleted.

**PgBouncer's admin console without client TLS,** for the fourteenth review's ER5: PgBouncer 1.26.1, built from pgbouncer.org's `pgbouncer-1.26.1.tar.gz` (SHA-256 `00886a2994b4844cd37110970df777772cfc42a74c400522c82fba509a3f42d9`, checked against its `.sha256`), in transaction mode with `client_tls_sslmode` unset, which `SHOW CONFIG` reported as `disable`, the default, in front of a throwaway Postgres 18.6 with `ssl = off`, the Manjaro package, both under `.claude/scratch/` with `TMPDIR` there and their sockets under `~/.claude/scratch/`. The client was the same kind of probe, `tokio-postgres` 0.7.18 with `NoTls`, built offline from the workspace's `Cargo.lock`, sending `RECONNECT` as a simple query:

| Connection | `sslmode` | Result |
|---|---|---|
| Admin console, TCP | `disable`, `prefer` | connects; `RECONNECT` completes |
| Admin console, TCP | `require` | **fails:** `error performing TLS handshake: server does not support TLS` |
| Admin console, Unix socket | `disable`, `prefer` | connects; `RECONNECT` completes |
| Admin console, Unix socket | `require` | fails the same way, as the driver does on any socket |
| A pooled database, TCP | `disable` | connects; `SELECT version()` gives 18.6 |
| A pooled database, TCP | `require` | fails the same way |

So with `sslmode`'s default an admin URL over TCP to a PgBouncer without client TLS fails its send, `?sslmode=disable` on that URL sends, and a Unix-socket admin URL works once morm gives it `SslMode::Disable`, as it gives every socket host (R-1277, R-1258, T-0316). Not run: PgBouncer 1.20.1 and 1.22, which T-0316 runs, Postgres 15 to 17, and a PgBouncer with client TLS on; the servers and the probe were deleted.

### The stale-session state machine: evidence

The design pass ran on throwaway servers: Postgres 17.12, built from ftp.postgresql.org's `postgresql-17.12.tar.bz2` (SHA-256 `9cf136a703d8ee2f83465c43be4e9e8e7930c82b0bfba13be7e2537be769ac55`, checked against its `.sha256`), and Postgres 18.6, the Manjaro package `18.6-1`, not built from a tarball; PgBouncer 1.20.1 (`24992cf557d73426d7048698dffc7b019e6364d4d8757ae2cf5e2471286a2088`) and 1.26.1 (`00886a2994b4844cd37110970df777772cfc42a74c400522c82fba509a3f42d9`), built from pgbouncer.org's tarballs and checked against their `.sha256`, one of each in front of each Postgres, in transaction mode with three server connections and `server_round_robin = 1`, 1.26.1 with `max_prepared_statements = 200`. Clients were `psql` and a harness on `tokio-postgres` 0.7.18; server sessions were told apart by `pg_backend_pid()`. 17.12 and 18.6 printed the same apart from source line numbers.

| # | Claim | Result |
|---|---|---|
| X1 | A `42804` from plpgsql's parameter routines needs no schema change | Yes: a function reading `e1.%I` into a record, given a table whose `v` is `int` and then one whose `v` is `bigint`, gets `42804` from `plpgsql_param_eval_recfield` on every switch, with no DDL. So the caller's arguments can pace a "certain" signal (N1, closed by MB) |
| X2 | A `0A000` from `RevalidateCachedQuery` needs no schema change, and fresh text doesn't stop it | Yes: a statement prepared under `search_path = t1` and run with `set_config('search_path', 't2', true)` gets `0A000`; one prepared under `t2` fails the same way under `t1`; unnamed runs answer under either (MD) |
| X3 | Behind PgBouncer that `0A000` reaches other clients and arrives at prepare | Yes, on 1.26.1 in front of both: a client preparing the same text, or a salted copy, under the other path gets `0A000` at prepare (N2) |
| X4 | A `22P02` from stored text that names no retired type behaves as a stale one | Yes: converting a stored `_morm_retired_x` gives `22P02` from `enum_in` on old and new connections alike, and succeeds on both once the row changes |
| X5 | A connection opened after a change compiles plpgsql fresh | Yes: after a relabel swap, a connection that had called the function fails on every call; one open but never calling it, and one opened after, answer right. Connection age never calls a stale session fresh (MA) |
| X6 | Closing one connection lets the next open pass a role's `CONNECTION LIMIT` | Yes: the third open at limit 2 gets `53300` from `InitializeSessionUserId`; after a close it succeeds |
| X7 | Which server session a transaction reaches behind PgBouncer | Sequential transactions from one client reached one session 30 of 30 on both versions, even with `server_round_robin = 1`; three overlapping clients reached all three, 10 each (N5) |
| X8 | Plpgsql left stale in one server session while the others answer | Yes, on all four pairs: the session that compiled the function failed 10 of 10, the other two answered 10 of 10 each |
| X9 | Behind PgBouncer new text cures a stale server statement, and can go stale again | Yes, on 1.26.1: after a hand-made drop, named runs got `XX000` from `getTypeInputInfo` 6 of 6, unnamed runs and a salted text were right; a second drop made the salted text fail and a new salt cured it |
| X10 | `RECONNECT` heals both kinds of stale state | Yes: on 1.26.1 the three server pids were replaced and clients preparing the stale text were right 18 of 18 and 6 of 6; on both versions the session X8 left stale answered right afterwards 15 of 15 |
| X11 | PgBouncer bounds the server statements salts leave | Yes: 300 distinct texts through one 1.26.1 client left exactly 200 in each server session's `pg_prepared_statements` |

Not run: PgBouncer 1.22 (proof 16 ran 1.22.0 and 1.22.1 with 1.26.1's results), Postgres 15 and 16 (the building steps' tests run every version), a Postgres 18 built from a tarball, and morm itself, which has no runtime yet. The machine ran as a prototype, about 800 lines of stable Rust 1.99.1 with a seeded generator, against a model the rows above calibrate: 3,000 seeds of 4,000 events for each of nine variants, straight and behind PgBouncer, with abusive clients, pending entries off, caps of 1 and a long `stale_cooldown`, about 100 million calls, with no failing seed. Broken one way at a time (no gate, no epoch, PR #3's ungated `42804` raise, an epoch that never ends, no replacement on an old connection, a dropped deferral, no fresh run inside the window), it was caught by the invariant meant for each. The prototype found two gaps in the design's first draft, N3 and N4, both closed in it. All servers, the harness and the prototype were throwaway and are deleted; the design file holds every result.

### Re-run in CI for the second review (EF9)

The spikes came back from `d8bd613`, as step 02 did under DE, with one new spike and new rows in proof 18, and ran in `spikes.yml` on the `postgres:15` to `postgres:18` images, Postgres 15.20, 16.16, 17.12 and 18.7, with PgBouncer 1.22.0 and 1.26.1 (one server connection, `max_prepared_statements = 200`), a streaming standby and `postgresql-NN-plpgsql-check` 2.10.16-1.pgdg13+2. Every proof printed "every check passed" on every version: the push run at `4ae3a1e`, https://github.com/biggaloy/rust-morm/actions/runs/43472484941, and again at `b682836`, the last commit with the spikes, https://github.com/biggaloy/rust-morm/actions/runs/43473420754, each job `spikes (pg NN)`, steps "Stale-statement design (p19), straight", "... PgBouncer 1.22", "... PgBouncer 1.26.1", "... standby" and "Proof 18". GitHub keeps the logs for 90 days, so what they show is here.

- **`p19`, SA and SB.** A session caches statements and calls functions before SA's swap (rename the type to `_morm_retired_m2_st`, create `st` as `('c','b','a','d')`, retire the column, add the new one, change `n` to `int8`), which runs straight on the primary as the type's owner, a role that isn't superuser, in four variants, then runs each again. It ran with the old session straight, through each PgBouncer, and on the standby, where the swap and every `ALTER` replay. Every cell was the same in all of them, on all four versions:

  | After the swap | No relabel, no reset | Relabel only | Reset only | Relabel and reset |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | `(($1::st) < ($2::st))::text` with `'a'`, `'c'` | `true`, **silent** | `22P02` naming `_morm_retired_m2_st` | `false` | `false` |
  | `('a'::st < 'c'::st)::text` | `true`, **silent** | `true`, **silent** | `false` | `false` |
  | `INSERT ... WHERE 'a'::st < ($1::st)` with `'c'` (not on the standby) | inserts, **silent** | `22P02` retired | no row | no row |
  | a free `(($1::st))::text` with `'a'` | `a` | `22P02` retired | `a` | `a` |
  | a function `RETURN ('a'::st < 'c'::st)` | `true`, **silent** | `true`, **silent** | `false` | `false` |
  | a function and a procedure with `DECLARE v st; v := p;`, `v < 'c'` | `true`, **silent** | `22P02` retired | `42883` | `22P02` retired |
  | a function with two such variables, `v < w` | `true`, **silent** | `22P02` retired | `true`, **silent** | `22P02` retired |
  | a `t%ROWTYPE` function, `(r.n + 1)::text` | `42804` (`plpgsql_param_eval_recfield`) | `42804` | `2` | `2` |
  | a cached `SELECT n FROM t` | `0A000` | `0A000` | `0A000` | `0A000` |

  With the relabel, the retired column read `_morm_retired_a` and `_morm_retired_c`. Then the owner ran `ALTER FUNCTION ... COST 100`, the cost it had, on both functions, and `ALTER PROCEDURE ... SECURITY INVOKER` on the procedure, already `INVOKER`: each wrote a new `pg_proc` row (`xmin` and `ctid` changed), and in the old session, straight, through each PgBouncer and on the standby once replayed, the function, the two-variable function and the procedure each returned `false`, the new order's answer. `ALTER PROCEDURE ... COST 100` failed with `42P13 invalid attribute in procedure definition`.
- **A `READ COMMITTED` transaction open across a swap** with the reset and no relabel, straight: `'b' < 'c'` read `true` before, `true` after the swap committed in the same transaction, and `false` once the transaction had read another table.
- **Proof 18's pragma rows**, the table above, including EF3's escaped ones: the same on all four versions.

The steps that build each change keep permanent tests of it on Postgres 15 to 18.

The spikes and `spikes.yml` were deleted before merge, as the step says, first after step 55's own run, whose last commit with them is `d8bd613`, and again after the second review's run; the last commit that has them is `b682836`: `git checkout b68283690a95055ec7e4429a3533b61125e3b54e`. It holds `p19` beside proofs 13 to 18.
